| b_breach alone, 22 tiles of round trip carried both ways (11 each way; DA D.7's 'about 11 tiles each way'; report-only reading) | 1,056 | 176 | 52,800 | [169,800, 170,100] | 236 to 237 | above the warn edge by >= 79,800 |
| the Suit gate, 34 tiles of round trip carried both ways (17 each way; 34 x 8 = 272 runs <= 274) | 1,632 | 272 | 81,600 | [141,000, 141,300] | 366 (302 of a full reserve) | above the warn edge by 51,000 to 51,300 |
| withdrawn 56-tile round-trip ceiling (STRESS ROW, over the bound) | 2,688 | 448 | 134,400 | [88,200, 88,500] | 602 to 603 (497 of a full reserve) | warn edge crossed at run 442 to 443 of 448, so the traverse ENDS STRAINED, not CRITICAL; CRITICAL (`k0` + HOLD_12 100 = run 542 to 543) arrives 94 to 95 runs into the return pod leg (Suit D.1 table: R0 <= 107,400 reaches CRITICAL inside a nominal leg); FAILED unreachable (needs run >= 892) |

These are tiles of round trip; Suit's 'each way walking out and carrying back' figure of 22 tiles is a different reading (22 x (4 + 8) = 264 runs) and is the gate *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*.

B's nested worst case (`b_pump` also decompressed, 40 tiles round trip) read by legs (walk out, carry back) is 20 x (4 + 8) = 240 runs = 72,000 mGU, left [150,600, 150,900], <= 274: it passes; read as carried both ways it is 320 runs and exceeds the gate, so DA's lint must read it by legs (DA request). **Drop-and-return** (a second identical traverse): 22 tiles of round trip 352 runs, 105,600 mGU, left [117,000, 117,300] (above the warn edge); 34 tiles of round trip 544 runs, 163,200, left [59,400, 59,700], warn edge crossed at run 442 to 443, CRITICAL reached on the last runs (542 to 543 of 544), not empty, home arrival after the 157 to 158-run return leg [12,000, 12,600], FAILED unreachable; 56 tiles 896 runs, 268,800 > arrival, reserve gone at run `k1` = 742 to 743, Suit FAILED at `max(k0 + 100, k1) + 150` = 892 to 893 <= 896, that is 3 to 4 runs (18 to 24 ticks) before the exit (EC-BS-38).

**`TOPUP_MODE` is VERB (user ruling X5).** A pre-sortie O2 `top_up` at a lit O2_TANK port is the EXPECTED play (Suit sink-and-faucet table: a top-up before every sortie is the expected rhythm, 270,000 mGU = 9 percent of the home tank); every figure here presumes the seat boards with a full reserve, and `top_up` can raise the reserve only to `RESERVE_MAX`, never above, so it cannot buy back the pod leg. A seat that skips it after a B sortie boards below the warn edge and reaches CRITICAL inside the outbound pod leg (Suit D.1 table).

**Reserve constraint (the lint is Suit's A15 / AC-SE-75a, not a Boarding constant):** `(RESERVE_S - 30) x 10 >= 2 x 163 + traverse_runs`. That needs `RESERVE_S` >= 63 with no traverse (Suit's range floor is 63 since A15, so the floor satisfies it with no traverse), >= 81 for 22 tiles of round trip (502 runs), and >= 90 for the 34-tile gate (598 runs; 2 runs spare at the default) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Routed: Suit owns the range and A15, Derelict Authoring DA-28 / D.7 owns `TRAVERSE_TOO_LONG`; Boarding states the arithmetic only. (The earlier `RESERVE_S >= 46` reading is withdrawn: it presumed a full reserve; the former range floor 45 is withdrawn too: at 45 the player arrives at 87,600 to 87,900 mGU, under the warn edge.)

**Open-door cost of the carry out:** leaving `door_a_spine_machine` open on the way in lets `K_OPEN` conduction cool `a_spine`, and the carry back through it is about 288 of the reference 624 ticks (`[ASSUMED anchor]`); that cost is Derelict Authoring's open-door projection printed beside every closed-door row by `COLD_ROOM_DRIFT` (DA D.18, AC-DA-40) and is cited here, not recomputed. *Design-time; DA's lints are the authority.*

### D.5 `cap_versus_leave`

The cap_versus_leave formula is defined as:

`leak_step(p) = floor(cap_p * OPEN_END_RATE_PM / 1000)` ; `steps = floor(t_s * TICK_RATE / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS)` ; `leak_total = (sum over open fluid ports of leak_step(p)) * steps` ; `share_pm = min(1000, floor(1000 * leak_total / n_room))` for `n_room >= 1`, and the sentinel `NO_GAS` (nothing left to lose, `leak_total` effectively 0) for `n_room = 0` (a vented room, `P_dkPa` 0), with `n_room = V * GAS_SCALE * P_dkPa` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* ; `cap_cost_ticks = n_stubs * CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS + T_EQUIP` ; `cap_cost_AU = n_stubs * CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS * U_TOOL`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cap_p` | int mGU/step (loop 1) or mL/step (loops 3 and 4) | scrubber 101: 400; O2 tank 103: 1200; vent 104: 600; pump 304: 40 (two); reservoir 303: 40; reactor 402: 2; fuel tank 403: 5; power-only modules: no fluid port | Item DB IDB-7 declared caps | the port's rated flow |
| `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` | int permille | 500 (100 to 1000) | Graph (registry: `OPEN_END_RATE_PM`; LSG:960) | the share of the cap that leaks per step until `cap_port` |
| `leak_step(p)` | int per step | scrubber 200; O2 tank 600; vent 300; pump 20 each; reservoir 20; reactor 1; fuel tank 2 | IDB-7 severed-leak column = IDB F-12's `floor(cap * OPEN_END_RATE_PM / 1000)` | already-halved: the table value **is** the leak |
| `t_s` | int s | 0 to about 480 | the session (WM `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` 28,800 ticks, 8 min) | time spent aboard with the stub open |
| `V`, `P_dkPa` | int tiles, int dkPa | `a_life` 30; A 118 in total; standard 1000 | DA C.2 and the Graph (`STD_PRESSURE_DKPA` 1000) | the room volume and its pressure |
| `GAS_SCALE` | int | 1000 mGU per GU | Graph (registry) | unit scale |
| `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS` | int ticks | 90 (60 to 180) | Interaction, owner's constant, not a standalone registry entry | one cap |
| `T_EQUIP` | int ticks | unregistered, 0 here | SN OQ-SN-6 | the welder swap |

**Output Range:** `leak_total >= 0` and 0 for power-only modules; `share_pm` 0 to 1000 (clamped), and over a full safe sortie (`t_s` 480, 4800 steps) **32 permille for the scrubber stub on `a_life` (30 tiles)**, 48 for a vent stub and 96 for an O2-tank stub in a 30-tile room, scaling as 30 / `V` in a smaller room *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (the earlier "0 to about 20" held only to `t_s` 300); `cap_cost` 90 ticks (1.5 s) and 360 AU raw per stub, plus the swap. The decision is the boolean *cap* and is the player's; the formula only prints the prices.

**Example:** `scr_a`'s stub on `a_life` (`n_room` = 30 * 1000 * 1000 = 30,000,000 mGU at standard pressure, `[UNV: DA authors A's rooms breathable]`): 200 mGU per step, 10 steps per second. A 60 s linger loses `200 * 600` = 120,000 mGU = **4 permille (0.4 percent)**; 150 s loses 1.0 percent; 300 s loses 2.0 percent. Against `R_BREATH` [PLACEHOLDER] 300 mGU per step per crew (Suit-owned, cited by the Graph) the stub is two-thirds of one person's breath. Capping costs 90 ticks and 360 AU. **Finding (RK-7, recorded and not repaired):** on a derelict you leave, the leave cost within one sortie is negligible, and a departed derelict goes dormant so the leak stops (LS-17, WM-24); capping pays for the **weld donor** only, because a welded section's `open_end` ports would keep leaking into the home body: an uncapped 200 mGU scrubber stub against the home 3,000,000 mGU O2 tank (IDB F-14) loses `200 * 600` = 120,000 mGU = **4 percent per minute**, and an O2-tank stub (600) 12 percent per minute (answered by the Graph, LSG:794: a welded section's stub leaks from the home network's pool once the seam valve is opened, and from the section's own stock while it is closed; the two figures assume the valve open) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. No pressure is invented to make capping matter (BS-8).

### D.6 `modules_per_fuel`

The modules_per_fuel formula is defined as:

`hop_ok(pool) <=> pool - HOP_FUEL_ML >= HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` (a hop from HOME; WM D.4) ; `hops_max(t) = max(0, floor((F0 - burn(t) - HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML) / HOP_FUEL_ML))` ; `burn(t) = floor(P_W * FUEL_UML_PER_W * 600 * t_min / 1,000,000)` mL ; `modules_max = hops_max` at one carried module per seat per hop (BS-12)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `F0` | int mL | 20,000 at wake (`ft_main`) | WM D.4 (the Graph's FUEL_TANK `stock_ml`) | the home pool; no MVP refuel source (WM-20) |
| `HOP_FUEL_ML` | int mL | 400 (100 to 2000) [PLACEHOLDER] | Web Map WM-20 | debited on departure from HOME only; the return is prepaid (`RETURN_HOP_FUEL_ML` kept 0) |
| `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` | int mL | 1500 (1440 to 4000) [PLACEHOLDER] | Web Map WM D.5 | the pool that must remain after the debit |
| `P_W` | int W | 0 to 2500 | the Graph (home draw) | the reactor's load |
| `FUEL_UML_PER_W` | int | 120 (40 to 400) | Graph Tuning Knobs, not a registry entry (LSG:958) | burn per watt per step |
| `t_min` | int min | 0 to about 100 | the session | elapsed campaign time |

**Output Range:** `hops_max` 0 to 46 (no burn); a `berth` is a separate `BERTH_FUEL_ML` 800 [PLACEHOLDER] debit (WM-21).

**Example:** no burn: `floor((20000 - 1500) / 400)` = **46**; the 46th hop is accepted at pool 2000 (`2000 - 400 >= 1500`), the 47th refused `FUEL_LOW` at 1600. With the reactor at full draw (2500 W, 180 mL per minute, WM D.5): after 30 min `floor((20000 - 5400 - 1500) / 400)` = 32; after 60 min 19; after 90 min 5; after 100 min 1; the last hop is accepted at a burn of 18,100 mL, about 100.56 min. One hop debits 400 mL = 2 percent of the tank = 2.2 minutes of full-draw burn. **Finding (retracts the part-1 claim, C-BS-14):** fuel is a campaign clock shared with the reactor and bounds the late campaign; it does not bound a sortie, and it is not the cap on a haul. Three useful modules from A cost three hops, 1200 mL, 6 percent of the tank. The pressure on a haul is the one carried instance, the slow walk and the tell. The MVP end-to-end script (home to A, home to B, berth B, weld, A again) debits 400, 0, 400, 0, 800, 400 = 2000 mL, 10 percent of the tank (WM D.4). *All values [PLACEHOLDER] (Web Map); revisit at the kill/continue playtest.*

### D.7 `check_cost_ratio`

The check_cost_ratio formula is defined as:

`C_check = ceil(REACH_PX / V_WALK) + T_EQUIP + FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` ticks (SN D.7, cited) ; `check_ok <=> 20 * C_check <= T_home` (equivalently `C_check <= 0.05 * T_home`, SN-21, cross-multiplied so it stays integer) ; `T_home(a) = carry_ticks(px_to_console)` ; `T_home(b) = T_home(a) + HOP_LEG_TICKS + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` ; `T_home(c) = T_home(b) + route_ticks(px_home, carry, n_home)` ; `d* = (FIELD_CHECK_TICKS / TICK_RATE) * (V_WALK * TICK_RATE / TILE_PX)` tiles (SN-18, cited)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `C_check` (SCAN) | int ticks | 156 (2.6 s): 36 reach + 0 swap + 120 | Sanity SN D.7 and AC-SN-70 | the cost of a SCAN, a lower bound |
| `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` | int ticks | 120 (90 to 240) [PLACEHOLDER] | Interaction IA-30 | the lit channel | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
| `T_home` | int ticks | readings (a), (b), (c) | Sanity: "the one-way trip home with the item" (SN D.7); the endpoint is unstated | the trip the check is weighed against |
| `px_to_console`, `px_home` | int px | D.1 legs | `[ASSUMED anchor]` | the carry in the derelict and at home |
| `d*` | int tiles | 5 at `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` 120 | Sanity SN-18, AC-SN-70 | a witness nearer than 5 tiles is cheaper to look at than to scan |

**Output Range:** `ratio = C_check / T_home` from about 0.05 (pass) to 0.25; pass is `ratio <= 0.05`, that is `T_home >= 3120` ticks (52 s) for a SCAN.

**Example:** `htr_a`'s route (624 ticks): reading (a) 624 ticks, ratio **0.250**, fails; (b) with the 945-tick hop, 1569, **0.099**, fails; (c) with the 486-tick home carry, 2055, **0.076**, fails. DA's 28-tile one-way carry bound (1344 ticks): (a) 0.116, (b) 0.068, (c) 2775, **0.056**, fails narrowly. **Finding (C-BS-11):** a SCAN passes Sanity's 5 percent test on no MVP route under any reading; for reading (c) the carry alone would have to be at least `3120 - 945 - 486` = 1689 ticks (35 tiles), for reading (a) 3120 ticks (65 tiles, past Suit's vacuum budget `VACUUM_BUDGET_S` 27 s (22 tiles each way walking out and carrying back, 34 carried both ways; AC-SE-75a); A has no unbreathable stretch, so it binds B only) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — confirming review, Suit R6 pod derivation)*. It is not a defect at the MVP, because class (d) ships no junk: Sanity itself says an item that near "is not worth a trip and the check is not the point" (SN D.7). It is a constraint on VS-A junk placement: any junk a SCAN is meant to disprove must sit at least 3120 ticks of `T_home` from the console on the chosen reading, and DA's bounds must not be tuned to satisfy this. The cheap rung is the breath-step (pick up and feel `cue_load`, `C_check` is the pickup), valid only while no stowed class masks the carried one (`held_mass_cue`, `STOWED_CUE_FLOOR_CLASS` [PLACEHOLDER]).

### D.8 `sortie_dwell_envelope`

The sortie_dwell_envelope formula is defined as:

`runs_aboard = floor(t_aboard_s * TICK_RATE / SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR)` (Sanity's run is 12 ticks, 5 per second) ; `dwell_msu = runs_aboard * (ABOARD_EXPOSURE - REC_IDLE)` while standing quietly ; `t_stage1_s = ceil((SAN_MAX - STAGE_ENTER[1]) / (ABOARD_EXPOSURE - REC_IDLE)) / 5` ; the envelopes nest iff `t_target_s <= t_stage1_s` and the cell and reserve are sufficient *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(the rate is `net_aboard = RING_EXPOSURE[ring] + ABOARD_EXPOSURE - REC_IDLE` (the ring term is 0 at ring 1, so the ring-1 figures below are unchanged); `t_stage1_s` is defined only for `net_aboard > 0` and is the sentinel `UNBOUNDED` otherwise (Sanity's registry rule (e) keeps `net_aboard` positive and under `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN`, so the sentinel is a guard and not a reachable case); the second-sortie row is `L_start(2) = L_end(1) + settled_runs * (REC_SETTLED - RING_EXPOSURE[ring])` and `t_stage1_s(2) = ceil((L_start(2) - STAGE_ENTER[1]) / net_aboard) / 5`, with `settled_runs` `[UNV: Sanity, whether the pod leg and the home body count as settled]`)*
**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_aboard_s` | int s | 0 to about 480 | the session; counted from `pod_settle` to departure, the pod leg is not aboard (SN-5 E1) | time off the home body |
| `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` | int mSU per run | 20 (8 to 20) [PLACEHOLDER] | Sanity SN E1 | the dwell exposure |
| `REC_IDLE` | int mSU per run | 1 | Sanity (registry: `REC_IDLE`) | quiet recovery aboard |
| `SAN_MAX`, `STAGE_ENTER[1]` | int mSU | 100,000, 70,000 [PLACEHOLDER] | Sanity (registry: `SAN_MAX`, `STAGE_ENTER`) | the level scale and the stage-1 entry threshold |
| `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` | int ticks | 12 | Sanity | one drift run |
| `t_target_s` | int s | 300 (design target, not registered; 180 to 300 [PROVISIONAL: creative-director], Tuning Knobs) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | BS-20 | the upper bound of the median first-sortie *W_ABOARD* (arrival to the departing console press, D.1); the concept's five-minute loop is the W_LOOP figure, the single target (BS-20), whose design-expectation W_ABOARD share is about 259 s (lint-guaranteed about 229 s) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, so 300 s here is the ceiling checked against stage 1 and not a second target *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` | int ticks | 28,800 (8 min) [PLACEHOLDER] | Web Map D.5 | the fuel-floor safe sortie |
| `CELL_S` | int s | 1200 (600 to 2400) [PLACEHOLDER] | Suit | the lit-beam cell |

**Output Range:** `dwell_msu` 0 to 45,600 for 8 min (2400 runs at 19); the ordering check is boolean.

**Example:** `t_stage1_s = ceil(30000 / 19) / 5` = 1579 / 5 = **315.8 s (5.3 min)**, as Sanity states (SN:344, a playtest hypothesis) `[UNV: Sanity re-runs its D.4 and D.8 under the X11 band change; STAGE_ENTER[1] is not changed by that ruling as read here]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. A sortie of 300 s W_ABOARD spends `1500 * 19` = 28,500 mSU, **95 percent** of the way to stage 1; an 8-minute one enters stage 1 at 5.3 min and spends 2.7 minutes in it. The orderings: design target 300 s <= stage-1 315.8 s (nests, with 15.8 s of slack) and stage-1 315.8 s < Web Map's safe sortie 480 s < `CELL_S` 1200 s. The fuel floor therefore tolerates a longer sortie than the sanity budget does; that is acceptable (the world shows the sanity cost, not a timer) and is recorded as C-BS-12 for Web Map and Sanity. The autosave loss ceiling (340 s, 5.7 min: SL's `loss_max` 20,400 ticks, D.9) is a different axis *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Second sortie.** The nest above holds for a first sortie from `SAN_MAX`. After a 300 s W_ABOARD the level is 100,000 - 28,500 = 71,500 mSU, 1,500 above stage 1; a player who goes straight back out reaches stage 1 after `ceil(1500 / 19) / 5` = 79 / 5 = **15.8 s**. If the pod leg and the home walk and carry (about 1,431 ticks, 119 runs) count as settled, `119 * 12` = 1,428 mSU come back, `L_start(2)` is 72,928 and stage 1 arrives after `ceil(2928 / 19) / 5` = 155 / 5 = **31.0 s**. Restoring the full first-sortie envelope takes `28,500 / 12` = 2,375 settled runs = 28,500 ticks = **475 s (7.9 min)** at home. So the envelopes nest for a first sortie and for a sortie after about 8 minutes at home, and not for a second sortie in a row; this is a pacing finding for Sanity and Web Map (C-BS-12), not a Boarding rule, and it is routed, not repaired. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* A long sortie that crosses 315.8 s enters stage 1, and that is **intended** (Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20), D73 part (c); the earlier band assumed it was not): stage 1 is a priced consequence of dwelling, and the median first sortie still nests below it. *Not hashed.*

### D.9 `loss_window_after_cut`

The loss_window_after_cut formula is defined as:

`t_eligible = max(t_cut_done, t_last_gen + AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS)` ; `t_save = first AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS poll at or after t_eligible with the SL-5 gate open, never later than t_eligible + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` ; `lost_cut_window = t_save - t_cut_done` ; baseline without the read: `lost_cut_window_interval = t_next_interval - t_cut_done (+ defer)` ; death side (SL, cited): `loss_max = AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS + R_g + DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` with `R_g` = 600 (the refractory-rounded warning window) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_cut_done` | tick | the tick of the V8 completion | Interaction (`last_salvage_T()`, ASK-BS-I4 answered) | when the cut finished |
| `t_last_gen` | tick | the last generation's `T` | Save/Load | when the world was last saved |
| `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | int ticks | 1800 | registry (Save/Load) | the minimum gap between interval or event generations |
| `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` | int ticks | 10,800 | registry | the interval trigger |
| `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` | int ticks | 7200 | registry | the longest a soft gate defers before a `RISKY` write |
| `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` | int ticks | 300 | Save/Load SL-5 | the gate re-evaluation period |
| `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` | int ticks | 1800 | registry | the downed time before death |
| gate states | set | hard: DOWNED, DEAD. Soft: row (a) endogenous Graph tell (EXOGENOUS cut descriptors open none); row (b) Attention tell with fewer than `W_MIN_WARNING_S * TICK_RATE` = 600 ticks left (FAIR mode, provisional); `GATE_SOFT_STATES` CHANNELLING, STAGGERED, IN_POD; row (c) any bound seat's `vitals_state` at CRITICAL or worse (user ruling X7 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*); the death restore also steps back one generation when `T_downed - g_r.T < W_MIN_WARNING_S x TICK_RATE`, and a post-DOWNED refractory of 600 ticks applies | Save/Load SL-5, SL-8 | what defers a due save; `CARRY_WALK` is not a gate state |

**Output Range:** with the read, `lost_cut_window` is 0 to `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` plus a gate deferral of at most `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` (0 to 9000 ticks, 1800 + 7200); without it, up to `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` = 18,000 ticks (5 min) before the next interval generation, inside SL's death-side ceiling `loss_max` = **20,400 ticks (5.7 min)**. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*

**Example:** the last generation was written at tick 7600 and the cut completes at 10,000: age 2400 is over the 1800 gap, so the cut is eligible at once; the player is IDLE or `CARRY_WALK`, neither a gate state, so it is written at the next 300-tick poll (window at most 300 ticks, 5 s) and a death on the carry restores to *after the cut*. A cut that completes 600 ticks after a generation waits 1200 ticks (20 s) for the gap; if the player is capping (CHANNELLING) or a tell has under 600 ticks left, it defers further. A save deferred 7200 ticks is written `RISKY`, and a death within `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` 3600 of restoring a `RISKY` generation steps back one generation (SL-8), so a doomed save cannot trap the player. Without the read, a cut 10 s after a generation waits for the interval generation 10,200 ticks (170 s) later, plus any gate deferral (at most `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200 ticks, 120 s; 18,000 ticks, 300 s, in total at the worst). A whole 5-minute sortie therefore fits inside one worst-case loss. *Design-time.*

### D.10 `lamp_cell_budget`

The lamp_cell_budget formula is defined as:

`cell_share_pm = min(1000, floor(1000 * lit_ticks / (CELL_S * TICK_RATE)))` ; `sorties_per_cell = floor(CELL_S * TICK_RATE / lit_ticks)` for `lit_ticks >= 1`, and the sentinel `UNBOUNDED` for `lit_ticks = 0` (a dark sortie spends no cell, so the quotient does not exist) ; `sorties_to_empty = ceil(CELL_S * TICK_RATE / lit_ticks)` at a constant lit cost per sortie and no refill between sorties (the sortie in which the cell reaches 0) ; with the ruled `top_up`: `cell_after_refill = min(CELL_S * TICK_RATE, cell_left + refill_ticks)`, where `refill_ticks` is the credit of one lit bare-hand TAP at a home BATTERY port, never more than the deficit (Suit D.9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(the `lit_ticks = 0` guard and the clamp are the review's; `sorties_to_strand` is renamed `sorties_to_empty` because an empty cell no longer strands, BS-19; the refill term is Suit's, cited and not defined here)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `lit_ticks` | int ticks | 0 to `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` 28,800 for a safe sortie (a lit sortie may run longer; the clamp covers it); realistic play 1,278 (the reference line) to 18,000 (a 300 s survey) | the player's beam use (`toggle_beam`, SE-8 to SE-10) | ticks with the chest lamp on while aboard and, because the statuses run in the pod (user ruling D60), ticks with the lamp left on during a hop, counted as 6 ticks per lit run (the statuses run in the pod, D60, R6): a nominal 945-tick leg holds 157 or 158 runs, so a fully lit leg debits 942 (N_lo) to 948 (N_hi) lit ticks, up to 978 on the 975-tick slack leg (163 runs); Suit drains GRAPH_STEP_TICKS per lit run (SE-8), Web Map WM-17 conforms *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `CELL_S` | int s | 1200 (600 to 2400) [PLACEHOLDER] | Suit | seconds of lit beam in a full cell; 72,000 ticks at the default |
| `RESERVE_S` | int s | 90 (63 to 240; narrowed from 45 to 240 by Suit's A15) [PLACEHOLDER] *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | Suit | engaged reserve breath, spent in an unbreathable stretch AND in the pod (the pod cabin is ANOXIC, Suit D.1 pod derivation), 47,100 to 47,400 mGU per nominal leg (D.4) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `GLOWSTICK_START_COUNT` | int | 2 (0 to 4) [PLACEHOLDER] | Suit | sticks in the start kit; each lights 150 s (`GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S` [PLACEHOLDER]) |
| `refill_ticks` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | int ticks | 0 to the deficit, per `top_up` TAP | Suit D.9 (`cell_deficit_ticks`, `CELL_STOCK_PER_KTICK` [PH]) | the cell credited by one lit bare-hand `top_up` at a home BATTERY port; the stock it withdraws is the home battery's (a store, not a power draw, so invisible to Attention) |

**Output Range:** `cell_share_pm` 0 to 1000 (clamped), and 17 to 400 over the realistic and safe-sortie `lit_ticks` above. `sorties_per_cell` is `UNBOUNDED` at `lit_ticks` 0, **56 at the reference line** (28 to 112 across `CELL_S` 600 to 2400), 4 at the 300 s survey and 2 at the 28,800-tick safe-sortie bound. The earlier range "1 to about 56" and the earlier `lit_ticks` bound of 18,000 were wrong at both ends.

**Example (FIXTURE-312, the 13-tile pre-ruling schedule; see D.2 fixture convention):** the reference line from arrival to the console with the beam on throughout: `312 + 60 + 240 + 12 + 624 + 30` = 1278 ticks (the 12 is the pickup dwell and the 30 the console approach, both fixture dwells of the D.2 golden schedule) = 21.3 s: `floor(1000 * 1278 / 72000)` = **17 permille** of `CELL_S`, `floor(72000 / 1278)` = 56 sorties. A survey sortie lit for 300 s (18,000 ticks) is **250 permille**: `sorties_per_cell` 4 and `sorties_to_empty` 4. *Built-route reading (indicative, the ruled 28-tile route, D.1):* `672 + 60 + 240 + 12 + 1,344 + 30` = 2,358 lit ticks = 39.3 s, 32 permille, 30 sorties. **Beam left lit across both nominal pod legs** adds 1,884 to 1,896 ticks (2.6 percent of `CELL_S`): the fixture reference line 1,278 becomes 3,162 to 3,174 (`cell_share_pm` 43 to 44, `sorties_per_cell` 22) and a 300 s survey 18,000 becomes 19,884 to 19,896 (276 permille, `sorties_per_cell` 3, `sorties_to_empty` 4). The default expectation is beam off in the pod (Suit AC-SE-36a runs with the beam off); the lit-leg cost is the player's choice *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Each stick thrown aboard adds 180 AU raw (`U_LOUD` [PLACEHOLDER]) to D.2's intake. A 30-minute session of two derelict sorties in the survey style spends about half the cell; the MVP refill is V7 `top_up` at a lit home BATTERY port (user ruling X5, D70b) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **Campaign reading** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: a lit-beam player who surveys at 300 s per sortie empties the cell in about four sorties **between refills**, and then chooses between refilling at home with `top_up`, going dark deliberately, or spending sticks (the authored sticks of the A route, 2 + 2 + 4 = 8, each 150 s, B's shelves not counted). No choice strands them, because the dock console is lit by the pod's status glow whatever the cell (BS-19). The dark-dock strand is therefore no longer a campaign-length event; AC-BS-92 still runs on the campaign-length strand cell, now to prove that the glow holds the invariant. Lighting less (a deliberate dark walk with `toggle_beam`) saves cell and costs sight of the frost sill. *Design-time; the home ship's lamps are fixtures, not the chest lamp.*

## Edge Cases

*Format.* Each case is `If [condition]: [exact outcome]. [rationale]`, ends in its owner's rule-id (Boarding adds no behaviour, BS-1, so an outcome is always an owner's), and carries its id `EC-BS-n` at the end of the bold condition. Cases are grouped by theme; the audit additions of the checkpoint (K15: a DESTROYED node, a store module, a restore in `IN_POD`, the stowed plate, the door that mixes cold) are EC-BS-3, EC-BS-4, EC-BS-40, EC-BS-45 and EC-BS-20, and the dead-bus case (K4) is EC-BS-17. Numbers are design-time and rest on D.1 to D.10. Web Map's constants are registered [PLACEHOLDER] values (see the Format note of Core Rules).

### A. Zero, maximum and out of range

- **If the player presses `begin_cut` with the welder in hand (`START_EQUIPPED_SLOT` 2) or with no cutter in hand (EC-BS-1)**: the verb answers `NO_TOOL` as an outcome after the press, nothing is cut and no tool-channel tick accrues; `equip_tool` to slot 1 is the fix and its duration is unregistered, so every sortie time stays a lower bound (BS-2 step 1; IA-5, SE-11, SN OQ-SN-6). The wrong tool is an honest, cheap failure, not a hidden gate.
- **If the player presses `begin_cap` while carrying, or tries to `equip_tool` the welder with hands full (EC-BS-2)**: both are `BUSY`; the cap is a bare-handed welder act, so the order is cut, cap, then pick up, which follows from "never auto-carried" and not from a rule (BS-8, BS-9; IA-28, SE-11). A second `begin_cap` on an already capped stub is `INVALID_TARGET` (IA-26).
- **If the node being cut is DESTROYED (EC-BS-3)**: the cut yields `scrap_count` of its repair part and **no module**; there is nothing to carry, the parts are STOWED stock (they ride the body, not the hands), and the cut's noise is spent regardless. A DESTROYED machine is the loudest thing in its room and was never worth a module (IDB-17, LSG:772; K15).
- **If the module being cut is a store (the bus battery `b_a2`, the lamp battery `b_a1`) (EC-BS-4)**: its `stock` is discarded into the Graph's `discarded_*` counters so the conservation invariant still closes, and a reinstalled store starts at stock 0 (IDB-18 (i)). A battery has no fluid port, so it leaves no stub; the island re-solves on the next run and what ran on it goes dark, `b_a1` being the stored energy behind A's one residual lamp (DA-27, `[derived]`), so cutting it ends the visible unknown and is a cost the player caused (IDB-18, K15).
- **If a second module is wanted while one is carried (EC-BS-5)**: `pickup` answers `BUSY`; the player drops the first in a safe room and shuttles, or leaves it for a second hop, and a second hop costs a second `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] (D.6). The spare `mod_breaker_junction` and `htr_a` cannot ride in the same hop (IA-28, SE-12).
- **If a stowed stack cannot take the pickup (a glowstick shelf of 2 with 3 already stowed, or a second `part_hull_plate`) (EC-BS-6)**: the pickup is rejected with Suit's immediate `STACK_FULL`, nothing is taken and nothing is deleted; the stack stays on the shelf and the player throws or uses one first. `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` [PLACEHOLDER] 4 and the plate's `stack_max` 1 are the structural bound (IA-28, SE-12, IDB-15).
- **If the player holds a tool in the HAND holder when a CARRIED-class yield is picked up (EC-BS-7)**: the pickup is admitted, because "needs hands free" means no carried instance and a held tool is a different holder (SE-12). Recorded as an ask to Interaction to confirm the wording (ASK-BS-I5); until then the sortie tests treat it as admitted (IA-28).

### B. The carry-admitted set, doors and drops

- **If the console row is refused and the player presses the pod console while carrying (EC-BS-8)**: the press is `BUSY` with no prompt; the module must be dropped first and stays aboard exactly as left, persisting on the dormant derelict, and the carry-home branch is lost. **This state is not shippable for the MVP (BS-9):** it removes the carry home, the loop the MVP exists to test, so the producer treats the refusal as a stop and not a degrade. With a BREACH running it would be the abandon cost, but the failure escalates to the producer as a design blocker and is not an accepted state (BS-9 fallback; C-BS-1; IA-5, WM-24).
- **If the carried def is not in the focused hardpoint's `compatible_defs` (EC-BS-9)**: there is no prompt at all, an immediate answer that reads only the carried def and the hardpoint's declaration and never a module-body field, so it is not an oracle (BS-18; MSA-26, IA-11).
- **If a FAILED-`hp` instance is installed (EC-BS-10)**: it installs as a FAILED module, `condition_bp` carrying through unchanged, and the home island treats it as dead; no verb refused the install and no reason named the condition, and the consequence is visible in the Graph's channel (a dark lantern, a dry trail, no output) (BS-18; IDB-17, IDB-18, LS-22).
- **If a CLOSED door lies on the carry route (the home `door_cargo_s`, or a door the player shut behind them) (EC-BS-11)**: the door face shows its ordinary V4 plate and `set_opening` is Applied (not `BUSY`); the carrier presses it, waits `DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` 30 [PLACEHOLDER] and walks through with `carry_ref` unchanged; a door shut as a containment tool is the player's own cost on the way out; during a TELL or BREACH the legal alternative is to abandon the module (BS-10, BS-13; IA-5, IA-24, IA-28). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*
- **If every candidate cell for a `drop` is blocked (a threshold square, nine blocked cells) (EC-BS-12)**: `drop` answers immediate `OBSTRUCTED` and the module stays carried; the player steps back a tile and tries again. Door cells answer `NONE` from `floor_tile_at`, so a drop never lands on one, and A and B corridors are 3 wide, so the risk cells are door thresholds only (BS-10; IA-28, DA-7).
- **If a SYSTEM-origin close targets a door whose leaf overlaps a carrier's foot box (EC-BS-13)**: the attempt becomes `CLOSING_BLOCKED` and retries every tick, landing on the first clear tick; the carrier reopens it with the ordinary press (BS-10) at `DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` they did not choose. MVP content has no SYSTEM close on a carry route, and a content change that adds one costs a carrier that surprise, which is why it stays an ask (BS-10; IA-24, ASK-BS-I2). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*
- **If a module is dropped and its section is then welded or cut away before the pickup (EC-BS-14)**: the loose item's position is `(section_id, pos_px)` section-local, so it goes with its section; a section that is cut away takes it with the Graph's `discarded_*` bookkeeping, a loss that belongs to the cut's owner (BS-23; LY-22, LS-16).

### C. The cut, the stubs and the faucet

- **If the machine being cut is running (the home scrubber, `htr_a` on a woken bus) (EC-BS-15)**: the cut is legal with no special case; the island re-solves on the next run, its dependants go STARVE, and the hum of the thing being cut drops: true-state audio in the consequence channel (LSG:976) that is not in LSG:44's enumerated list, used as true-state corroboration and not the falsifier of record until the Graph rules. The player who cuts their own air has made a P1 choice the world shows at once (BS-7, BS-4; LSG:773, FT:207, C-BS-4).
- **If `scr_a` is cut and carried home (EC-BS-16)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: it is a legal cut whose carry ends at a vacant scrubber hardpoint (the committed content route, user ruling D72), where `install_module` is offered by the same plate whether the instance is FAILED or healthy (no oracle, IA-11) and installs it as what it is: a FAILED `scr_a` installs a FAILED module with no take-back (no `remove_module`, MSA-28), in a hardpoint that a healthy module could have filled. That is the stake of the read, and the reference cut stays `htr_a` (BS-17; MSA-26, MSA-28, IDB-14, C-BS-3).
- **If `scr_a` is read on A's dead bus (EC-BS-17)**: every lantern is dark, `scr_a`'s and `pump_a`'s alike, and the room's O2 and temperature cannot tell an unpowered healthy scrubber from a FAILED one; the closed-list witnesses falsify "running" and nothing more. A SCAN gives an honest reading (`FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], scanner in hand), and waking the bus with the spare breaker lights `pump_a`'s lantern while `scr_a`'s stays dark, at the price of the pump-stall clock (90 s) and wear doubled by `DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION`. The world told the player it was dead; SCAN or the woken bus tells them it was broken (BS-17; DA-24, DA-25, AC-DA-111, K4). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The woken branch buys about 12.9 minutes of unhurried work in the lethal room (DA D.22); the user accepted that modest window as the payoff for waking it (D45), and Derelict Authoring's X-DA-8 reasons log may later trigger one authored payoff that only a served branch delivers. Attention's locus-body pass condition for derelict A is option (b), a fault that wakes when its bus is served (`j_a_main` once fed), confirmed under D47.
- **If a stub is never capped, across a restore (EC-BS-18)**: `stubs[]` persists, the hiss persists and nothing forces anything; the leak is a standing consequence-channel truth, bounded by dormancy on a departed derelict (BS-8; IA-26, LS-17, WM-24).
- **If two seats target the same node or the same stub (co-op hook) (EC-BS-19)**: the second `begin_cut` is `BUSY` (one job per `(subject_ref, verb class)`), and a second `begin_cap` on a capped stub is `INVALID_TARGET`; the first lit `pickup` of the yield wins and the other seat's is `BUSY` or finds the item gone (BS-7; IA-34, IA-26).
- **If the player opens `door_a_spine_machine` and leaves it open (EC-BS-20)**: the "one lethal room" statement is a build-state invariant, so opening the door lets conduction mix the cold into `a_spine` (authored `T0` -12000 mC) for as long as it stays open; the lint prints the open-door projection, the tell stays the frost fan over the sill, and shutting the door behind (a bare-hand V4) is the player's containment act, with its cost on the way out (BS-10; DA-28, DA D.18).
- **If a cut node is cut again (EC-BS-21)**: the slot is a SALVAGED tombstone and the verb answers `NOT_SALVAGEABLE`; salvage-once is the faucet's whole shape, and the remaining salvage in a room is read from the empty pedestal rect, the scorched kerf and the stubs, never from a count (BS-16; IDB-17, LSG:243, LY-20, ART 7.4).
- **If an abandoned module is revisited on a derelict that went dormant (EC-BS-22)**: the module is in `loose[]` where it was left and the derelict stands as left; if it was dropped in the dark it needs light to re-take, and a dormant room's temperature and gas stand still until the player returns (BS-13, BS-16; LS-17, WM-24, WM-25, WM-28, IA-28).

### D. Tells, breach and retreat

- **If a tell opens as the cut begins (EC-BS-23)**: the cut completes (240 ticks is well under the played draw), leaving `T - 240` ticks; at the shortest played draw (`TELL_DRAW_LO` 900 [PLACEHOLDER]) a 13-tile carry fits by 36 ticks and a 14-tile carry does not, and at the 600-tick fixture floor 7 tiles is the most; drop-and-run always fits. The cut is loud and forfeits the stillness abort while it runs (BS-7, BS-13; D.3, AT-8, AT-10). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Under the ruled carry intake the sortie's own tell opens 318 ticks into the carry *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* and not as the cut begins (D.2), so this case is the fixture-armed contract case (`attention_arm_tell`).
- **If a tell opens during the carry and the route is longer than the window (EC-BS-24)**: the decision is the player's, keep walking to the console or set the module down and run at `V_RUN` 3 against `V_CARRY` 1, and both are legal; the module persists where it fell, and re-taking it needs light. A drop is free (exempt from the lit rule), so a false alarm costs time and not the haul (BS-13; IA-28, PC-8, AT-8). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(the decision is the ruled shape: the tell opens inside the carry, holding cargo never outwaits it and a drop does; its margin is thin, 140 permille of the tell draw at 28 tiles, and the pass line is the boarding spike's, D.2, D.3, OQ-BS-27)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **If a BREACH is running and the carrier reaches the console (EC-BS-25)**: the hop is always allowed, `carry_ref` travels, and the departure ends the BREACH when the last recorded occupant leaves a non-home locus body; the hop pushes no stimulus and does not interrupt a TELL. The hop is the escape verb with the module still in the arms (BS-12, BS-13; WM-26, AT-22).
- **If a horror is met during a carry (EC-BS-26)**: nothing kills it, and the MVP kit has no repel tool (user ruling X6; HE-39 is dormant), so the answer is retreat, stillness, darkness or 'drop the cargo', which the user confirmed as the intended price of hauling (D63: in Horror's figures a carrier who keeps the cargo is struck on 7 of 18 draws, with `R_SNAP_PX` 48 the lever) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Horror places nothing in salvage and has no edge to author (HE:376), so no rule here changes with a horror's presence (BS-13, BS-24). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21)* The lengthened decision carry runs through `a_spine`, a single 3-wide corridor with no side step, and a horror may meet the carrier there (Horror E-HE-59, answering ASK-BS-H1): the answer is the ruled set (drop and walk back, stillness and darkness, shut a door behind, BS-13) at the confirmed price (D63); no bypass or wider spine is asked.
- **If the player cancels a cut and goes still to abort a tell (EC-BS-27)**: the tell aborts after 594 ticks from an entry at `theta` and 630 from the level ceiling, against a ledger that keeps the partial for `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] 600 inclusive; the partial survives only an immediate resume at the lowest entry levels, so the cut restarts from zero. Restraint costs the partial and keeps the module in place (BS-7; D.3, IA-16, AT-10). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(once the cut has completed there is no partial to lose; under the ruling freezing is priced: with the instance held the carry-state intake keeps `L` above `theta_HOLD`, so the tell can be outwaited only after a drop, D.3, OQ-BS-27)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **If `pod_hop` applies with a channel open or a cut ledger standing (EC-BS-28)**: the open channel aborts `ABORT(BODY)` and the ledger entry is Interaction's; the leg and settle alone are 945 ticks, longer than `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] 600, so on return the cut restarts from zero `[UNV: Interaction to confirm the ledger expires on global ticks while the derelict is dormant]`. The module is still in place on the derelict (BS-12; IA-40, IA-16).
- **If the player hops out and back to shed Attention (EC-BS-29)**: the level is not reset (a hop does not interrupt a TELL and the departure ring is held), each outbound hop costs `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 400 mL, and a departure ends only a BREACH, not a tell or the level; a hop is a retreat and not a reset (BS-13; WM-26, AT-22, D.6). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* A hop that leaves a derelict locus before the due run does void that run's breach (TELL to CALM, `ABORTED`, rearm set) when every seat recorded at tell entry has gone, so leaving during the warning is the cheapest escape and costs whatever was not yet carried out; a home-ship locus is not voidable by hopping, and 'the lights you left on' is the price (user ruling D46, confirmed).
- **If the carrier hops home with the instance held after a tell on the derelict (EC-BS-63)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*: the burden stops when the seat enters the pod. It is pushed only while the salvage is held aboard the non-home locus body where it was taken, never `IN_POD` and never aboard the home body (delegated ruling R5, 'never to carrying at home', read with the creative-director's lean on the re-review; in the fiction, *the web hears the taking at the wreck*, BS-15), and the level then decays in the pod as usual. On the reference carry the tell is resolved before the pod arrives (from the tell's opening, the carry ahead, the console and the pod leg run-quantised to 948 ticks take 2,004 ticks, more than `TELL_DRAW_HI` 1800), by the departure void or the abort (AT-22, AT-10); the level arrives home at 75 percent of `ATT_THRESHOLD` (4,511,793 mAU) with the beam and the lit home ship feeding it in the pod, or 62 percent (3,734,036) with no pod intake, and stands at 4,389,820 or 3,796,361 mAU at the install after the 486-tick home carry: below `theta_HOLD`, with no home-ship tell (AC-BS-123 (e)). Were the burden to run in the pod and at home, the decay would stay suspended, the voided tell's item-keyed rearm would end on the first CALM run that drains the same item's push (AT-10), and a home-ship tell that no hop can void (D46) would open at once on every successful sortie that kept its module; that is why the scope is keyed on place. A shorter carry whose tell is still running when the pod arrives home is voided at its due run all the same, because every recorded seat has left the locus body (AT-22). Attention's AT-3 keys the push on the pickup locus and says 'never for carrying at home'; Attention's AT-3 row and F-26 state that gate (`actor_section` in a non-home locus body, not `IN_POD`); the Controller declares the push (PC-10, D.13 `burden_T`, AC-PCI-225, landed 2026-10-28, ASK-BS-C2 answered): `burden_T = U_BURDEN` iff `carry_ref != none AND salvage_carry AND body_of_actor(actor) != HOME_BODY AND loco_state not in {IN_POD, DOWNED, DEAD}`, pushed every tick, moving or not (Boarding's 'not `IN_POD` and not the home body' is the same set for a held carry; DOWNED and DEAD clear the carry within one tick, BS-13 e); the voided tell's item-keyed rearm is not cancelled by this carry, because no `BURDEN` push is drained in the pod or at home (AT-10) (D.2; AT-22, AT-10, OQ-BS-30) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*

### E. Light and the dark

- **If a module was dropped in the dark (EC-BS-30)**: it cannot be re-taken until something lights it; the player throws a stick before the pickup (throwing is `BUSY` while carrying, channelling, staggered or downed (IA-29), so after the drop it is allowed) or returns with the beam on. The stick lights at landing, never before (BS-5; IA-28, IA-29, IA-6); the throw pushes one `U_LOUD` [PLACEHOLDER] at landing, so re-lighting a dark drop is a loud act (IA-29, IA-33).
- **If both start sticks and the shelf are spent (`GLOWSTICK_START_COUNT` [PLACEHOLDER] 2, `a_dock` shelf 2) (EC-BS-31)**: a dropped module outside any light waits for the chest beam, cell permitting; the home airlock's four-stick supply is the resupply for the next sortie, and `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` 8 bounds how many live lights can stand at once (BS-5, BS-19; DA-26, EM, IA-29).
- **If the cell is exhausted mid-carry (EC-BS-32)**: the beam goes dark; carrying and `drop` need no light, but `pickup` needs light; the console press needs light too and the console is lit by the pod's status glow, so the module is carried to the dock and hopped home whatever the cell (BS-19, user ruling; SE, IA-6) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
- **If the cell is spent, no stick remains and the dock console would be unlit (EC-BS-33)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: under the ruling the console is lit by the docked pod's status glow, an ordinary lit fixture, so `lit(console)` holds and `pod_hop` is accepted (`NOT_LIT` cannot occur while the glow stands; a missing or misplaced glow is a build error of the placement lint, BS-19). The player still cannot re-take a module dropped far from the dock in the dark, which costs that module for the sortie and nothing more. Before the ruling this state was a true soft-lock reached by a long lit session (about 4 survey sorties spend the cell, D.10); the cell is now refillable at home by `top_up` (X5), and the mitigations that narrowed the old state (the two-stick shelf, the home four-stick supply, drop-throw-retake with one LOUD report per throw, IA-29) are no longer load-bearing for the hop. The state is covered by the strand cell of AC-BS-92, which must pass (BS-19, C-BS-9; IA-6, DA-26).
- **If the player turns the beam off by choice while carrying (EC-BS-34)**: `toggle_beam` is available as an actor verb; the carry spends no cell and the lamp's exposure term leaves the job, but the frost sill and the hazard are seen only in the beam, and the console needs light to press. A dark carry is a legal Pillar 3 trade, not a rule (BS-5, BS-9; PC-8, EM). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Beam switches are spaced by Emitter State's commit-spacing rule (`SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS`; Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20), D32, with no Lighting exception granted), so a flicker of the lamp is not a free probe; the reason word is Emitter's.

### F. Hazard rooms and death

- **If the actor is DOWNED in `a_machine` while carrying (EC-BS-35)**: the Controller clears `carry_ref`, Interaction converts the module to a loose item at the foot origin within one tick (its hook runs at divisor 1, IA-28), and `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800 starts (kept at 1800 for the VS-B revive); in the solo MVP a player may take the give-up-and-reload action while DOWNED (user ruling D7) and otherwise death restores from the newest autosave (SL-7, SL-8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, so the module is back on its pedestal if the newest autosave predates the cut, or at the kerf (loose or in the arms) if it followed the cut (EC-BS-42, D.9); either way the player's knowledge of the room is the carry-over. In co-op the loose module persists where it fell (BS-13; PC-9, SE-13, SE-24, SL-7).
- **If the player tries to strip `a_machine` (EC-BS-36)**: eight cuts of 240 ticks plus the crossing exceed the room's CRITICAL clock (2262 against 2184 ticks at `T0`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, so the room tolerates one purposeful cut and a shuttle and not a strip; the lethal cold is the authored cost of the richer room (BS-17, BS-20; D.4, DA D.10, DA-28).
- **If the bus is woken and then `htr_a` is cut (EC-BS-37)**: the cut is legal on a running machine; the room stops warming and relaxes back toward `T_AMB_MC`, the pump-stall clock (on `pump_a`) continues unaffected, and the heater that made lingering survivable is the thing in the player's arms. The player caused it and the Graph's channel shows it (BS-17; LSG:773, DA-28, DA:1163).
- **If a module is dropped in B's vacuum room and must be re-taken (EC-BS-38)** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — confirming review, Suit R6 pod derivation)*: the re-take is a second traverse against the reserve the player HAS, the arrival reserve 222,600 to 222,900 and not 270,000 (the outbound pod leg already spent 17.4 percent, Suit D.1). At 2,400 mGU per tile of carried round trip a drop-and-return costs 105,600 at B's authored 22 tiles (117,000 to 117,300 left, a survivable and expensive choice), 163,200 at the 34-tile gate limit (59,400 to 59,700 left; the warn edge is crossed and CRITICAL is reached on the last runs), and beyond the gate (the withdrawn 56 tiles) 268,800, more than the whole arrival reserve: the reserve is gone at run 742 to 743 and Suit's FAILED rung arrives at run 892 to 893 of 896, inside the vacuum. This is INTENDED and stated (Pillar 2, a legible punishing choice; delegated ruling R21 on the creative-director lean): the hiss, the draught, the valve click on the RESERVE edge and the breath cadence are the witnesses, and from the first body cue to FAILED is at least 25 s (Suit D.11). The lean 'expected to exhaust the reserve' holds for any route beyond the gate, which DA's lint forbids; at the gate limit it ends in CRITICAL, not empty (BS-20; DA-28, SE D.11, D.4).
- **If the actor's vitals fail while `IN_POD` (EC-BS-64)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the statuses run in the pod (user ruling D60, reversing the earlier hold of Suit's air, cell, thermal and counters), so a death in the pod is the Controller's DEAD (PC-9), `carry_ref` clears by the Controller's downed rule, and in the solo MVP the outcome is the restore from the newest autosave (SL-7, SL-8). A save in `IN_POD` stays a soft-gate state (EC-BS-40). In co-op the loose position of a module when a crewmate is downed in a pod is Web Map's and the Controller's to define (VS-B). The pod leg is part of the sortie's physical budget (D.4 for the reserve, D.10 for the cell; Suit D.1 pod derivation). A death in the pod is unreachable on any leg of 1,494 ticks or fewer (Suit: FAILED needs 250 runs), so it needs a long leg (HOP_LEG_TICKS up to 2190) and a low reserve (R0 <= 68,400 at a 2,265-tick leg) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*.

### G. Save, restore and autosave

- **If a restore lands mid-carry (EC-BS-39)**: `carry_ref` and the Suit's carried instance are restored together and cross-checked (`SUIT_RESTORE_CARRY_MISMATCH` on a mismatch), the Interaction `post_restore` never touches `carry_ref`, and a channel in progress is dropped (`RESTORE` abort; the Controller maps `CHANNELLING` to `IDLE`). A cut that completed before the autosave stands; one that did not is as if never begun (PC-17, SE-13, IA-15, IA-28).
- **If a restore lands in `IN_POD` (EC-BS-40)**: the mid-transit state follows Web Map's restore rules (the persisted leg parameters), `carry_ref` is restored with the Suit's instance, and the module is still in the arms on arrival; a save in `IN_POD` happens only after `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` as a `RISKY` write because `IN_POD` is a soft-gate state (BS-21, BS-12; SL-5, WM-30, PC-17; K15).
- **If an autosave lands mid-carry (EC-BS-41)**: it is legal, because `CARRY_WALK` is not a gate state; the restore point is the carry, with the cut done and the stubs hissing, and the loss window of a later death is the carry only (BS-21; D.9, SL-5).
- **If the player dies after an autosave that followed the cut, or before it (EC-BS-42)**: after it, the world restores with the kerf, the hissing stubs and the module in the arms or on the floor, and the loss is the carry; before it, the node is intact, nothing of the take remains, and the player keeps what they learned of the room. Failure costs ship and body, never knowledge (BS-21; SL-7, SL-8, D.9, P5). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* After the restore the threat draws (the tell length, Horror's) are re-keyed per restore epoch (a death restore advances it; a Continue of the newest Save & Quit keeps it, so the draws replay identically; a Continue of any other generation advances it; Save/Load stores it in the per-slot `slot_epoch.bin`, staged before `request_restore`; delegated ruling R1 as amended by R13/R14) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R1 as amended by R13/R14)*, so a quit or a death cannot steer them, while the Graph's physical failures and the persisted counters replay exactly (user ruling X8, D16); the first RUNNING frame after a death restore carries one plain ship-log line (the HUD's FIRST_FRAME_LINE keyed on `restore_reason = DEATH_STUB`, no death wording; user ruling D15, HUD's and not Boarding's).

### H. Hallucination and the field checks

- **If a FAILED module's sprite or gauge reads alive (class (b), the MVP lie) (EC-BS-43)**: the module-body channel lies and the consequence channel does not; its lantern stays dark on a live bus, because lamp brightness is a closed-list witness (LSG:44); the decision is made on the closed-list witnesses and, where they cannot discriminate (the dead bus), on SCAN. There is no condition bar to trust (BS-6, BS-15, BS-17; LSG:44, LS-22).
- **If a phantom is reached for (class (d), VS-A) (EC-BS-44)**: a phantom has no subject and answers like an unlit object, so reaching for one is not a free oracle, and the junk that is real but dressed as a prize betrays itself by the breath-step, a SCAN, the palette leak or the trip home. Deferred with its blockers (BS-14, BS-15; EM-21, IA-13, SN-21).
- **If the stowed `part_hull_plate` is held (EC-BS-45)**: the plate lies in `a_spine` on the reference route, and a player who stows it masks the breath cue for the rest of the sortie, since `cue_load = max(carried_class, stowed_class)` reads HEAVY for any carried module; a pickup is `BUSY` while carrying, so the plate is taken on the way in or on a later hop. At the MVP the breath-step is perception only and records nothing, so no false verdict is stored; "drop the plate first" has no performable verb (ASK-BS-S1) (BS-11, BS-14; SE-28, SE OQ-8, DA-26, C-BS-10). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* With a HEAVY item stowed the actor cannot run (user ruling D18), so a drop-and-run retreat is a drop-and-walk at `V_WALK` 2 for a player who stowed the plate (D.3).
- **If a hallucination of an entity (class (a)) or of a crewmate (class (c)) appears during a carry (EC-BS-46)**: it hides the body and never the trail, and the human channel (a crewmate's beam colour and shape, their voice) stays true, so a false alarm costs a drop and a re-take under light, not the haul. Deferred to VS and VS-B (BS-15; SN-20, HE).
- **If any hallucination is applied to the cut's noise, the stub's hiss, the load's breath, the carry's pace, the time or the air (EC-BS-47)**: none can be; each price channel is truthful by its owner's rule, and a defect that let one lie is a P4 violation (BS-15; IA-33, LSG:44, SE-28, PC-8).

### I. Content, budgets and loaded sets

- **If the post-weld sortie to A (S4) is made (EC-BS-48)**: `S_int` is 144 authored subjects against `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` 120, over by 24 (DA OQ-DA-20), which the user's ruling D37 answers by registration by adjacency or proximity, so that only subjects near the actor register and the registered count stays inside the budget (Interaction owns the rule and the radius) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; Boarding seeds no loose item, raises no `P` above `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` 24, and a solo sortie holds at most one carried instance, so drops are one per module taken (BS-20; DA-32, IA D.10, C-BS-7).
- **If any sortie invariant is checked (EC-BS-49)**: it is checked over all five loaded sets S0 to S4 and both sortie orders, never only the first sortie to A: the route legs are per section and unaffected by what else is loaded, the subject budget is the one that moves (BS-20; DA-32, PF-5).
- **If the home pool is under the floor when a haul is installed or a hop is pressed (EC-BS-50)**: an outbound hop is refused `FUEL_LOW` but the module is installable at home; a return hop from a derelict is accepted at any pool, including 0, so no sortie strands a haul (BS-12; WM-20, WM D.4).
- **If the arrival cell at the destination is blocked (EC-BS-51)**: `pod_hop` answers `ARRIVAL_BLOCKED`, a defect of the dock's clear cell (`SPAWN_BLOCKED` lint); the module stays CARRIED, the player stays at the console and no cargo state changes (BS-2 step 2; WM-9, WM-15, DA-3).

### J. Co-op hooks (Vertical Slice B; solo first)

- **If a second and third seat board (EC-BS-52)**: each seat has its own `carry_ref` and its own stowed stacks, pod capacity is the seats (one at MVP, WM-5; the VS-B count is the concept's co-op range, owner Web Map at VS-B), and the haul is a division of labour; nothing in this GDD keys on a single seat (BS-12; PC-8, SE-12, WM-5).
- **If a crewmate is DOWNED while carrying (EC-BS-53)**: the Controller clears their `carry_ref` and the module falls at their foot origin; there is no stealing from a crewmate's hands and no reward for harm, and a revive is Interaction's and the Suit's (BS-24 G-8; PC-9, SE-13).
- **If a cutter is swung near a crewmate (EC-BS-54)**: a cut channel targets a SALVAGE_MODULE subject and never an ACTOR, so nothing is harmed by accident. Harm to a crewmate is not a choice a sane player can make: it needs a deliberate lit `begin_harm` channel (IA-31) **and is admitted only while the attacker's sanity is system-verified below the harm threshold** (Sanity's `actor_sanity_below_harm_threshold`, which reads the SIM `harm_eligible` bit; a sane actor's attempt is `NOT_ELIGIBLE` and does nothing; SN-11); **the victim sees the attacker's palette leak and sanity tell before eligibility opens** (`tell_on` leads `harm_eligible` by `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S`, SN-10); **and the incident is attributed to the web** (Interaction's call to Sanity's Class B mutator `record_harm_event(actor, victim, tick)` at harm-channel completion, SN-13), not to the player. Friendly fire is lethal by the user's decision ("death is a bitch"), but only the web's work can open it (Pillar 4(c); anti-pillar: no intentional PvP; BS-24 G-1 and G-8; IA-25, IA-31). Vertical Slice B: at MVP (one seat) no target exists.

### K. Degenerate strategies

- **If the player strips a wreck node by node (EC-BS-55)**: each module is a carried trip, a hop and a walk, the faucet is the authored node list and salvage-once, the left derelict persists as left, and a hazard room is lethal at eight cuts; there is no safe farm and no refill (BS-16; DA-21, IDB-17, IDB-29, D.4).
- **If the player cuts several modules in one visit and carries one per hop (EC-BS-56)**: the noise is Attention's whatever the carry (a chain of cuts trips a tell in the fifth cycle with the beam on, D.2), the yields lie where they fell and the derelict persists; the stockpile on the deck is a cost the web hears and not a store the player owns (BS-7; IA-33, D.2).
- **If the player stands still aboard to wait out a threat (EC-BS-57)**: listening is a stance and idle pushes nothing, but dwell is Sanity's price: 19 mSU per run net, stage 1 after 315.8 s; there is no refuge in waiting (BS-4; PC-10, SN E1, D.8).
- **If the player tries to cut in the dark to dodge exposure (EC-BS-58)**: a dark cut is impossible, the act gate needs the target lit, and a lit job is a visible job in the exposure ledger (BS-5; IA-6, EM).
- **If the player seals doors behind them to contain what follows (EC-BS-59)**: it is a legal Pillar 3 act with a cost on the way out (a closed door met while carrying, EC-BS-11); nothing in Boarding forbids it and no content closes a door for them (BS-10; IA-24).
- **If the player waits between the cut and the lift (EC-BS-66)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*: nothing is held, so the decay runs and the level the lift starts from falls (the yield lies on the deck while the player waits in `a_spine`; the printed sweep treats the wait as idle and `REF_GOLDEN` regenerates it with the walk) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; at 28 tiles, lit, FIXTURE-312, waits of 0, 300, 900 and 1800 ticks leave live shares of 140, 120, 93 and 66 permille (any lit wait floors at 26), and an 1800-tick wait in the dark leaves none (the tell opens 480 ticks into the carry with 864 ahead, inside every played draw). This is intended: **patience before the lift is legitimate restraint** (P3), and it is priced by the existing rules and not by the burden: Sanity's aboard dwell (19 mSU per run net, 2,850 mSU for a 30 s wait, D.8), the lethal cold if the player waits beside the yield in `a_machine` (D.4), the lamp cell if lit (D.10), and the time anything else has to arrive. The burden does not cover a yield within reach; the decision it prices is the carry's. K3 is asserted over the lit sweep, and the dark wait is reported and not asserted (D.2 K3; AT-5, F-26).
- **If the player drops the module at the tell, outwaits it and re-takes the same module (EC-BS-67)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*: the abort completes 810 ticks after the drop standing still lit (600 at the pre-glow dark floor), and the rearm keys on the item (Attention AT-10): re-taking the same module before `rearm_until_T` ends the rearm on that run, so there is no fresh `TELL_REARM_TICKS` immunity; the burden and the suspended decay resume and the tell re-opens about 156 ticks into the resumed carry (`rem` 870 at 28 tiles, so the second tell fits every played draw), and waiting out the whole rearm first only moves the re-opening to about 228 ticks (`rem` 798). This is intended: the player trades about 13.5 s of stillness, Sanity's dwell and the risk of what comes in that time for a carry that fits, which is restraint priced in time and not the cheap freeze the ruling set out to price (X3). A different item leaves the rearm in place (BS-13; AT-10, F-26).
- **If the tell opens with the carrier on a door cell or still inside `a_machine` (EC-BS-68)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: a `drop` never lands on a door cell (Interaction's `drop_placement`) and answers `OBSTRUCTED` when every candidate cell is blocked (EC-BS-12), and standing in an open doorway or inside `a_machine` spends the lethal-cold budget (D.4, EC-BS-20), so the drop-and-outwait answer is worse there than in the open spine. **Computed onset (2026-10-28)** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: measured as distance along the carry route past the CENTRE of `door_a_spine_machine` (DA C.2 (420, 504)); the carry starts at the cut point, in-room leg 204 + d with d = `REF_CARRY_CUT_OFFSET_PX` 24, so 228 px, and `V_CARRY` is 1 px per tick, so tick = px. The tell onset `t_x` = 318, 336, 360, 384 (lit pre-lift dwells 0, 300, 900, 1800) lies 318 - 228 = 90, 108, 132, 156 px past the door centre (1.9 to 3.25 tiles; 66 to 132 px beyond the far face if the door cell is one 48 px tile deep, DA to confirm); the beam-off, home-lamps-off, both-off and dark-1800 variants (`t_x` 348, 432, 468, 480) lie 120, 204, 240, 252 px past it. If the built route's longer 732-tick walk-in lowers `t_x` (the main review estimates `live_pm` about 180/160/120/80, i.e. `t_x` about 282 to 372, [UNV], not an oracle) the lit onset moves to about 54 to 144 px; the envelope across both conventions is 54 to 156 px. So the lit tell opens 1 to 3 tiles north of the doorway, inside the open-door cooled stretch (about 288 of the reference ticks, DA D.18) and next to a door cell on which `drop` answers OBSTRUCTED: this IS EC-BS-68's bad case. Derelict Authoring is asked to put a decision reach there (ASK-BS-D4 (d), OQ-BS-31) (BS-13; IA-28, DA-28).

### L. Simultaneous rules

- **If the cut completes on the run in which the level crosses `ATT_THRESHOLD` and the tell opens (EC-BS-60)**: both happen, because the cut's effect lands at completion only (IA-25) and the tell is drawn at entry from its own stream (AT-8) and neither owner reads the other; the player meets the tell with the yield on the deck and not in the arms, and the choice of D.3 (carry, or drop-free run) is available at once. The LOUD one-shot is part of the intake that opened the tell, which is the "weigh the noise" cost made visible (BS-7; IA-25, IA-33, AT-8).
- **If the player cancels a cut on the same tick the actor is staggered or an abort cause fires (EC-BS-61)**: `cancel_channel` wins and the hook finds no record, so the ledger entry is written once by the cancel and the abort cue is not played (IA-15); the stagger itself keeps the module (BS-9; PC-8, PC-9).
- **If a tell's abort condition and its breach-due tick fall on the same run (EC-BS-62)**: the abort wins, one transition, and no breach opens; a player who is below `theta_HOLD` for `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] at that run is saved by the margin they earned (BS-13; AT-10).

## Dependencies

Rows list only what the consumer must supply or read at the boundary (the quoted items of section "Interactions with Other Systems"), not a guess at a neighbour's internal state. **Direction** is from this GDD's side. **Strength** is the index's for the five direct rows (Dependency Map position 11) and this GDD's judgement for the indirect reads. **Status** is the `> **Status**` header of each file, re-read on 2026-10-27 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(at authoring, 2026-10-26, Perception Filter and Adaptive Audio & Tells were In Design)*: *Designed* means locked and cited by rule-id; *In Design* means the contract is provisional. Boarding & Salvage is a leaf: no designed system depends on it, so there is no cycle.

| # | System | Direction | Strength | Interface (the boundary item) | Status | Counterpart row confirms this edge |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Interaction System | this depends on it | **Hard** (index) | `pickup`, `drop`, `begin_cut`, `begin_cap`, `field_check`, `throw_glowstick`, `place_loose`, `loose[]`, `stubs[]`; `act_gate`; the abort set; `push_stimulus` | Designed | IA:464 and IA:830 (the reverse is "depended on by, Soft"; the two describe the same edge from either end and are not a conflict) |
| 2 | Suit & Equipment | this depends on it | **Hard** (index) | the carried instance, stowed stacks, tool slots and `equip_tool`, `cue_load`, `RESERVE_S` [PLACEHOLDER], `CELL_S` [PLACEHOLDER], glowsticks | Designed | SE:471, SE:978 |
| 3 | Modular Ship Assembly | this depends on it | **Hard** (index); *MSA's own row says Soft* — C-BS-8 | `install_module`, the seam cut as an extraction service (weld, seam cut and install are all MVP, user ruling X1 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*), `last_result`, `last_shape_change_T()`, the carve-out C1 | Designed | MSA:502, MSA:1023 (**strength differs**: the reference sortie ends in the install, so this GDD keeps the index's Hard and routes a refresh to MSA and the index) |
| 4 | Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits) | this depends on it | **Hard** (index) | `loose_slots`, module placements with `def_id` and `hp`, `entry_room_key`, the seeded loose ledger, dried trails, the hazard-room bounds, the five loaded sets | Designed | DA:680, DA:1229 |
| 5 | Item & Module Database | this depends on it | **Hard** (index) | `salvage_yield`, `carry_class`, `mass_g`, stub leak rates, parts, `T_cycle`, field-check inputs | Designed | IDB:469, IDB:863 |

**Bidirectionality audit.** For rows 1 to 6 and 8 to 13 the counterpart text exists and agrees on the edge; the disagreements are wording, not edges (the "Boarding owns placement" family and the strength of row 3) and are listed in Cross-References. Row 7 (the Graph) now has a counterpart row (LSG:281, LSG:880) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; row 15 (Horror) names Boarding but expects nothing; row 16 (Emitter State, 2D Lighting, Command & Simulation Layer) and the Failure & Trail edge name none in the reverse direction and are **accepted one-directional edges**, because Boarding registers nothing they could read (BS-1); row 17 (Perception Filter, Adaptive Audio & Tells; now Designed) is consistent from their side (PF:550, PF:1149; AA:262, AA:553, AA:1083), and row 17b is consistent for HUD & Suit Layer, In-World Instruments and Chart Table. Row 11: Web Map is Designed and its row WM:414 confirms BS-12, BS-16, BS-19 and the asks; its constants are registered as [PLACEHOLDER]. **Order of design.** All five direct dependencies are Designed, so this GDD can be authored against locked text; a later change to any of them re-opens the specific rule listed in the row's interface cell.

**Blocking dependencies (ruled 2026-10-27).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The two USER DECISIONS that gated this system are ruled. **ASK-BS-I1 is answered** (unchanged). (1) **The hop-availability invariant (BS-19; OQ-BS-4)** is held by the docked pod's status glow: Interaction's refusal of ASK-BS-I3 stands (IA-6 is unchanged); Web Map accepts ASK-BS-W3 and reverses its ASK-BS-W2 answer; Emitter State's self-powered fixture class has LANDED (EM-3, EM-11, E10); Derelict Authoring places and lints the glow. Its rows stay BLOCKING for implementation (AC-BS-92, AC-BS-51b) until Web Map's and Derelict Authoring's parts land (Emitter's has) and the strand cell passes *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. (2) **The anchor's tell decision (OQ-BS-27)** is ruled as option B. What now blocks the MVP playtest and the tell stories is Derelict Authoring's lengthened reference route, AC-BS-123 green on the regenerated `REF_GOLDEN`, and the boarding spike's live-choice pass line for its assertion (d) (AC-BS-132); Attention's recalibration is run (F-26: weights unchanged, `U_BURDEN` 7 [PLACEHOLDER]) (D.2, X-BS-11, AC-BS-122, OQ-BS-10) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*.

## Tuning Knobs

**Boarding & Salvage owns no knob.** It registers no constant (BS-1, R1, R27), so this is a **cited-knob table**: every row names the **owner**, whose GDD and registry entry are the source of truth and where the value is changed; the default and the safe range are the owner's, copied for reading and never restated as Boarding's. The ★ mark of the template (a registry candidate, a cross-system fact this GDD introduces) is **used on no row**; the two *design targets* at the end are Boarding's own, are **not registered** and are not lints. Every value that the registry marks [PLACEHOLDER] carries the tag in its row. "Too high" and "too low" say what a sortie feels like at the extreme. Rows are grouped by sortie step (BS-2); "formula" names the Formulas row that uses the knob.

### Enter and leave (the hop)

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] | 900 | ticks | 360 to 2190 (HOP_LEG_TICKS_MAX lint) | the lull between boards stretches into a loading screen; the hop stops being felt as a crossing; the non-interactive share of W_LOOP passes its 40 percent ceiling | the commitment of the crossing vanishes; the lull that frames the held breath is lost | `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS`, D.1 `route_ticks`, D.8; **a pacing knob for W_LOOP** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Web Map (WM-34) |
| `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] | 45 | ticks | 24 to 180 | a long hush before the dock is live | arrival feels like a cut, not a threshold | `HOP_LEG_TICKS`, D.1 | Web Map (WM-14) |
| `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] | 400 | mL | 100 to 2000 | fewer sorties per campaign; the haul becomes a fuel puzzle, which the one-per-hop rule would then amplify | the debit is not felt (the Web Map moment-ordering tripwire) | `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML`, D.6 | Web Map (WM-20) |
| `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] | 1500 | mL | 1440 to 4000 | hops refused with usable fuel; the campaign clock tightens | the floor stops guaranteeing a safe sortie (below 1440) | `HOP_FUEL_ML`, `FUEL_UML_PER_W`, D.6 | Web Map (WM D.5) |
| `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] | 28,800 | ticks | per Web Map | the fuel floor tolerates a sortie far longer than the sanity budget (C-BS-12) | the floor is high for little reason | `ABOARD_EXPOSURE`, D.8 | Web Map (WM D.5) |
