### Design targets (Boarding's own; not registered; not lints)

| Target | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| First-sortie player time to A, *W_ABOARD* (arrival to console, the cut and carry-home of `htr_a`; D.1 defines the window; the concept's five-minute loop is the *W_LOOP* figure, about 41 s longer) | design expectation about 259 (lint-guaranteed about 229, OQ-BS-28 (d)) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, derived from the single target W_LOOP 300 s less about 41 s of pod legs and home leg; band 180 to 300, whose top is the stage-1 ceiling on the median and not a second target *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | s of player time | 180 to 300 [PROVISIONAL: creative-director]; the upper bound is capped below the sanity stage-1 point (315.8 s at the placeholders, D.8) so the median sortie does not enter stage 1; a long sortie that enters stage 1 is **intended** (Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20), D73 part (c) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*), as a priced consequence of dwelling, and the band concerns the median only (an 8 min median, 480 s, equals `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` and is well past it); enforced by DA's `DWELL_BUDGET_LOW` (OQ-BS-28 (d)) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21)* | the stage-1 point is crossed by the median, so the median sortie reaches sanity stage 1 | the sortie is a transaction, the held breath is lost | D.1 (route floor 60 s), D.8; tuned by route and node placement, never by `V_CARRY` | Boarding (BS-20), playtest |
| Reference-sortie tell rate (a tell met between arrival and the console press, beam on, normal dwell; X-BS-11) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | a tell is met in at least 3 of 5 sorties and, of those that meet one, at least 1 of 5 and at most 4 of 5 end in a drop, an abort or a BREACH rather than a plain outwalk [PROVISIONAL: creative-director; ceiling re-based 2026-10-27] | sorties out of 5 | 3 to 5 meet a tell; 1 to 4 end in a drop, an abort or a breach | every sortie ends in a drop or a breach: the carry is never a bet (and the old 'at most 4 of 5 meet a tell' ceiling is withdrawn: a lit carry now meets a tell by construction, D.2) | no sortie ends in a drop, an abort or a breach: walking on always wins and restraint is not a strategy (P3) | D.2 K1 and K3; moved by the ruling of OQ-BS-27 (option B: the carry burden and the lengthened carry; Attention's recalibration is run, F-26) and the boarding spike's pass line (AC-BS-132), not by one constant *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*; never by a Boarding constant | Boarding (the target), Attention and Derelict Authoring (the levers); a precondition of the MVP playtest |
| Non-interactive share of W_LOOP (the pod legs, 1890 ticks at the defaults) | at most 40 percent of the median W_LOOP [PROVISIONAL: creative-director] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | percent | 30 to 40; 35 percent at the 90.2 s survey allowance, 52 percent at the 60.2 s floor (D.1) | the loop is a ferry: the pod legs outweigh the sortie | the hop stops being felt as a crossing (Web Map's own tripwire) | `HOP_LEG_TICKS` (a pacing knob here as well as a fuel and fiction constant), D.1, X-BS-1 | Boarding (target), Web Map (the knob) |
| Minimum `T_home` for a SCAN-disprovable lure (VS-A) | at least 20 x `C_check` = 3120 | ticks | no lower | SCAN is meaningful | SCAN is a free oracle or a waste | D.7; DA's bounds must not be tuned to satisfy it | Boarding (BS-14), Derelict Authoring |

**Interactions worth tuning together.** `U_TOOL` x `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` x the node density of a room (DA-21) jointly set how many cuts trip a tell (D.2), and with `W_L`, `W_P`, `NOISE_PER_TICK`, `B_table`, the ruled `U_BURDEN` (with its decay suspension) and the carry length *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* whether, and how far into the carry, the reference sortie meets one (X-BS-11, D.2); `TELL_DRAW_LO` and `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` set whether the reference carry fits (D.3); `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS`, `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` and the dark floor set whether restraint keeps the partial (D.3); `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` and the design target set how much of the sanity budget a sortie spends (D.8). Because `V_CARRY` is locked, the only haul levers are route length and node placement (Derelict Authoring), the Attention placeholders, and the hop price (Web Map); no lever here may be a pressure the world does not show. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

## Visual/Audio Requirements

*Consulted: art-director (spec of 2026-10-27; its decisions AD-BS-1 to AD-BS-7 are adopted below and close C-BS-5). Citations: ART x.y = `design/art/art-bible.md`; HR-n = ART 7.7; IA V-n = Interaction's Visual/Audio event labels; FT = Failure & Trail; SE = Suit & Equipment; DA = Derelict Authoring; BS-n = this GDD's rules. Hex values are the bible's (ART 4.1, 4.6); relationships are the contract, not the hex (ART 4.0). Audio is specified as **existence and sync only**: the audio-director owns design, layers, ducking and mix (Adaptive Audio & Tells, now Designed; this section supplies its event list) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.*

### Lane (read first)

Boarding & Salvage registers no state, hook, command, mutator, read view or notification (BS-1), so **it produces no visual event of its own**. Every pixel in a sortie is drawn by an owner: the HUD (prompt, bar), the character and tool renderer, Interaction's V8, pickup and drop completions, the Graph and Failure & Trail (the medium at an open end, lamp brightness, room air), Suit (breath, visor fog), Assembly (the seat, the seam), Web Map (pod transit) and the 2D lighting stack (beam, glowstick pool, exposure). This section therefore does three things and only three:

1. **Art direction Boarding owns (BS-22):** the open-end pieces and cap pieces (kit gap K-9), the empty-pedestal kerf state, the one shared carried-module sprite and its pose, and the loose-yield sprite rules.
2. **Composition constraints** on visuals other owners draw during a sortie: what the sortie must never show (a value, a condition, a loot glow, a ghost, a meter) and what it must keep legible (the way home, the trail that outruns a carrier, the medium at an open end).
3. **Classification** of every sortie visual into the truth grammar (below), so Sanity's drift stages and the field-check ladder (BS-14) know what may lie.

Where an owner already specifies a visual, this section cites its row label and does not restate it; anything restated would be an ownership absorption and a drift risk. **Vocabulary guard (G-9):** "graft", "severed", "bleed" and "stump" are design prose only; no asset name, slice name, palette note, sprite brief, VFX brief or audio brief uses them (the one exception is the Graph's own descriptor name, which is a data id and never a sprite label). Asset tokens use `end`, `cut`, `cap`. The sprite brief for an open end reads "a cut pipe end", never an organ metaphor (ART 4.4: flesh and violet are reserved for sanity leaks; the ship is a body only in the player's reading, never in the pixels).

### Art decisions made here (adopted from the art-director)

| ID | Decision | Alternatives rejected | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| AD-BS-1 | **Carry pose: module held overhead.** Both arms raised, the module centred over the helmet, its bottom edge at least 2 px above the helmet crown, at all five drawn facings. **Closes C-BS-5.** | *Below the lamp*: at S, SE and E the module sits across the forward beam arc and the chest point and fails the ART 5.1 import lint by construction, and hides the lamp that is the player's only comfort (ART 2.6). *Hip-side hold*: three mirrored facings flip the side, so the module crosses the chest on the mirrored half. | ART 8.2's 80 x 80 cell was sized for "module above helmet" (ART:1041); ART 5.2 allows "below it or overhead, never across it" and wants the carry to break the outline upward (the 25 percent silhouette test reads "carrying" at a glance). |
| AD-BS-2 | **The carried sprite is capped at 24 px wide by 14 px tall**: figure (55 px including contour) + 2 px gap + module (at most 14) = at most **71 px**, under the 72 px wall front face (ART 8.2), so the module never reads above the wall cap when a carrier walks along a wall. | A scale-true carried module (up to 96 x 96): exceeds the 80 x 80 cell and covers the figure and the beam. | The shared sprite is a plain stand-in "drawn once, plainly" (ART 5.1); an abstraction of load, not a portrait of the part (BS-22). The scale lie is accepted (flag F-14). |
| AD-BS-3 | **Kerf shape: a linear ragged line along the mount edge, never radial.** Void Steel `#0B1018` to Graphite mid `#2A303A`, matte, hue-less, **no afterglow and no cooling-ember animation**. | A radial burn (reads as a power-arc scorch in greyscale, ART 3.3 "sharp, dark, radial", and confuses a cut mount with an arc fault). A cooling glow (an emitter the web can see that no system owns, ART Principle 3, EM). | The five trail behaviours must stay distinct in greyscale (ART 3.3). A kerf is history, not the state of a loop. |
| AD-BS-4 | **Open-end and cap pieces reuse the ART 8.5 grammar**: `<loop>_end_cut_<h\|v>` and `<loop>_cap_<h\|v>` slices in `pipe_shared_alb.png`; no new `stub` token. "Stub" stays a design noun. | A new `stub` token. | ART 8.5 already has the piece tokens `end` and `cap` and the state token `cut` (doors); only a one-line maintenance note is owed (ASK-BS-AD2). |
| AD-BS-5 | **Loose-yield sprites carry no condition variants.** One sprite per def (plus its dialect); no cracks, scorch, rust or "damaged" art that tracks `condition_bp`. | A wear-state sprite set per def. | BS-6 forbids any condition readout; a sprite wear state is module-body and corruptible (PF-5) and would be the inspect card in disguise. The honest differences live in the world (trails, lantern, room air). |
| AD-BS-6 | **Loose-yield sprite size follows the def's physical footprint** in the prop cells of ART 8.2 (48 x 48, or 96 x 48 for the largest tanks and the reactor cell). **Any future lure (VS-A junk def) uses the same cell set**, so size is not a real-versus-phantom tell. | Size keyed to mass class. | Mass is felt through the body cue (SE-28), never read off the sprite. |
| AD-BS-7 | **The loose sprite carries its loop's identity as a non-emissive flat albedo accent**: a 2 px stripe in the loop hue on the module's medium window, plus the loop's fitting silhouette and a static flow-chevron pattern, body Graphite. No emission, no glow, no outline. | A hue-less loose sprite (loses the vocabulary the player learned); an emissive accent (a dead module would be a lantern). | The literal reading of ART 4.2, "salvage is Graphite plus its loop hue", and Principle 2 (a dead loop is Graphite, no emission, static pattern). The accent is albedo, so it shows only under a beam or a stick. ART 4.2 does not say albedo or emission; the albedo reading is adopted (flag F-5). |

Steps are BS-2's.

### Animation and motion constraints

**The carry pose and its lints (AD-BS-1, AD-BS-2).**

- *Pose.* Both arms raised, the module centred over the helmet, 24 x 14 px at most, bottom edge at least 2 px above the crown; five drawn facings (S, SE, E, NE, N), three mirrored (ART 5.6); the module is symmetric, so a mirror is free.
- *Silhouette (tier 1, ART 5.6).* At 25 percent scale, no colour, no contour, the figure reads helmet circle + pack rectangle + a block breaking the outline *upward*. The module must not merge into the helmet circle (at least 2 px clearance), or it would break the "only perfect circle on a perfect rectangle" rule (ART 3.2).
- *Chest-lamp lint (ART 5.1, ART 8.9 item 18).* In every carry frame at every facing: no module, arm or limb pixel within 3 px of `anchor_chest_<facing>` (the chest point keeps `|x| <= 2`), and the beam axis ray from that anchor is clear of module pixels for its first 12 px. At S, SE and E the module is behind the beam; at NE and N the helmet and pack already occupy the ray and the module sits above the crown (about 20 px or more from the anchor by the ART 5.1 ratios). Measured against the real anchor slices before approval.
- *Height budget.* Figure 55 px + 2 px gap + module at most 14 px = 71 px, under the 72 px wall front face, so the module never pokes into the wall cap layer along a wall.
- *Contour and layering.* The carried sprite has a 1 px Void Steel contour like any hero object on the character layer (ART 5.1; supporting environment never does). It has **no loop hue and no dialect marks**: Graphite mid with Graphite lit plate edges, so every carry looks the same (closes IA Open Question 23 / CE-24). Tool layer above the figure; `anchor_carry_<facing>` supplies the per-frame offset (a new anchor name, ASK-BS-AD2).

**Locomotion (ART 5.2, stiff-weighted).** Carry is a walk variant of 8 frames, deliberate, no helmet bounce, no squash, no overshoot. Stride length matches `V_CARRY` (1 px per tick = 60 px/s): **zero foot slip at 60 px/s is an import lint** (ground contact measured per frame); the cycle is re-timed, not the walk cycle slowed. The pack's breath rise (2 to 3 px) continues while carrying; the load signature does not change the silhouette or the walk timing (mass never changes `V_CARRY`, IDB-21). Idle while carrying is the raised hold held still with pack breath only. Pickup and drop transitions are 2 to 3 frames each, no flourish, no ease-in-out beyond a held frame. No exaggeration except Controller- and Suit-driven stagger, recoil and collapse frames; **a carry stagger keeps the module** (PC-8): the module wobbles at most 1 px with the body and is never thrown, dropped or tossed by a stagger frame (a forced drop is a downed event only). Facing is locked during channels (five drawn facings).

**Cuts, not fades (ART 7.3 spirit).** The module-to-pedestal swap, the loose-sprite appearance, the pickup swap, the cap appearance and the open-end medium ending are all **single-frame cuts**; the optional VS settle on the yield is the only eased motion and has no overshoot. Persistent world motion in a derelict during boarding is limited to dust in the beam, the residual lamp (steady), open-end media (FT) and the player's own breath and steps (ART 2.6, "nothing else moves unless the player moves it"; ART 6.4: nothing decorative moves). **Nothing on or around a salvage node idles, bobs, pulses, glints or sparkles to attract the eye** (G-5, ART 6.4 cap). The HUD bar and prompt follow Interaction's motion list unchanged (no settle, tremor or glide on the bar; the glide is reserved for lying readings, ART 7.3). No HUD element flashes above 3 Hz (HR-13); no full-frame flash on cut completion, hop, breach or pickup.

**Reduced motion (HR-8) and photosensitivity.** Open-end medium propagation and the failure-trail animation are **never suppressed** (they are the truth channel); dust drift may slow; the carry cycle is unchanged; the Alarm sweep becomes a static mark plus sound (ART 7.3). The cutter and welder sparks obey the 3 Hz ceiling by construction (stepped, stochastic, localized at the grip anchor, never full-frame); the Chemlight pool wanes monotonically and never pulses. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Reduce flashing defaults ON at first launch (user ruling X10, D79), and the flash-counting unit (`ceil(n_transitions / 2)`), `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` weighted per area and the creative-director exemptions are Pixel Pipeline's and the HUD's (user ruling X9), cited and not defined here.

**What a "decide" frame is made of.** Per ART 6.5 ("spend light to read it") the three verdict sentences are distributed across rooms (cause in one, the crew's verb in another, absence in a third); an empty pedestal with a clean kerf is the **absence** sentence for a module the crew themselves cut, and a never-salvaged node is whole. Boarding adds one composition constraint to Derelict Authoring and the generator: **a cut mount must not be mistaken for a dead-ship feature.** The player's kerf (clean and linear, no soot cloud) must be distinguishable from an authored dried-trail scorch (radial arc, ART 3.3) at a glance and in greyscale; the distinction is geometric (line versus radial), not tonal.

### Truth-channel classification

Four classes, because the run rulings (R9, R10) and the dead-bus finding (K4) need a finer grid than "consequence versus module-body":

- **T, consequence channel of record** (the Graph's closed list, LSG:44): compartment temperature, O2 and pressure as the body feels them; frost, mist, scorch and drip trails; hiss, creak and whistle at a descriptor's anchor; lamp brightness (including a node lantern dark or lit); draught. Never lies; lies about it are not permitted. The falsifier of record.
- **A, anchor**: never shifts under hallucination (ART Corollary, HR-6): beam colour, shape and radius; the HUD frame, prompt plate and bar; the character sprites and hand tools; the shared carried-module sprite; filaments at the cone edge (attention is not sanity); glowstick pools; a crewmate's beam.
- **S, supporting, corroborating, not of record**: undisturbed dust and interrupted props (verdict grammar, ART 6.5); the hum of a machine (an Audio class, FT:207; true-state audio in the consequence channel, LSG:976, but not in LSG:44's enumerated list, so true-state corroboration and not the falsifier of record until the Graph rules, C-BS-4); the residual lamp; a dried trail on a module's own fitting where Derelict Authoring placed a dead fault (a candidate field check, never a guarantee, FT:71).
- **M, module-body, corruptible** (PF-5, LS-22): a module's sprite, its gauges and sight-glass faces, an inspect card, a module's own wear state, the loose-yield sprite, a door panel lamp (DA AD-11).
- **Authored structure and props (TRUTH_ST, PF-5 row 15, provisional)**: the pedestal rect and the empty shelf; the Filter may add phantom copies, never edit.

| Sortie visual | Class | Corruptible under drift? | Leak when it lies | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Beam, cone, radius | A | never | n/a | the true light radius is the fixed anchor; a false reach is a violet fringe on the rim, the radius is unchanged |
| Prompt plate, key-cap, state-neutral verb, bar, needle-mark, break-notch | A | never | n/a | IA R-10: unchanged through stages 1 to 3 |
| Carried-module sprite (shared) | A | never | none | an anchor that **does not discriminate** real from phantom (BS-22); the class (d) leak lives on the loose sprite, before pickup |
| Hand tools, work-loop frames | A | never | n/a | the same loop for every subject |
| Open-end piece (static fitting) | M-adjacent | trivial | at stage 3 the loop hue may leak violet at silhouettes like any pipe (ART 4.4) | the piece is never a witness; the **medium** is |
| Open-end medium (mist, frost fan, puddle) and its ending at `cap_port` | **T** | no | n/a | the falsifier for "this module is cut and leaking" |
| Hiss at the open end | **T** | no | n/a | on the closed list |
| Kerf (Boarding-owned art) | M | yes at stage 3 (veins) | violet at the silhouette edge | history, not state |
| Pedestal rect, empty shelf | TRUTH_ST (authored structure and props, PF-5 row 15, provisional) | never edited; the Filter may add phantom copies | n/a | history, not state |
| Loose-yield sprite | M | yes (class d: phantom) | violet then flesh at the outline fringe | no condition variants (AD-BS-5); same size cells (AD-BS-6) |
| Machine node sprite, gauge face, sight-glass | M | yes (class b) | violet gleam on the glass (stage 1), HR-5 tri-modal | the module-body channel |
| Node lantern / lamp brightness in the node's compartment | **T** | no | n/a | on a **dead bus every lantern is dark** (DA-24): `scr_a`'s and `pump_a`'s alike; once the bus is woken `pump_a`'s lantern lights and `scr_a`'s stays dark. So lantern brightness falsifies "running", not "FAILED versus healthy" on a dead bus (health is SCAN, or a woken bus) |
| Room O2 and temperature as the body feels them (breath, visor fog, rime, shiver) | **T** | no | n/a | Suit's body cue register (SE-25); cannot be faked |
| Draught (AIRFLOW streaks) | **T** | no | n/a | FT |
| Dust (undisturbed versus disturbed) | S | not designed to lie | n/a | supporting only (ART 6.5, VA-14) |
| Dried trail on a module's own fitting | S | n/a | n/a | exists only where DA placed a dead fault (FT:71); a FAILED scrubber leaves none (FT-18) |
| Interrupted props, empty suits | S | n/a | n/a | never a body (ART 6.5) |
| Residual lamp (one per derelict) | S / A | no | n/a | steady, never Attention-modulated; found dark on return is honest (DA-27) |
| Filaments at the cone rim (the tell) | A (attention is not sanity) | never | n/a | ART 2.7; a hallucinated filament may not substitute for the real one |
| Suit readings (O2, warmth, charge, SCAN readout) | M (lie-capable architecture, honest in MVP) | VS-A only | violet gleam + glide + sound (HR-5) | the MVP scanner is an honest anchor (SN-19) |
| Body cue: load breath (SE-28) | **T** | no | n/a | a pure function of true `mass_g` and suit state; cannot be faked, tinted or suppressed |
| Pod console, glyphs and prompt | A (prompt) / M (panel lamp) | the panel lamp may lie but must not change on the press | gleam | Web Map's and Interaction's; Boarding adds only the "in sight on arrival" content constraint |
| Pod status glow (the docked pod's self-powered fixture) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | A (it carries the way home; the Perception Filter adds its PF-5 row) | never | n/a | a hallucination that dimmed or moved it would remove the way home (BS-19), so it must be an anchor; its luminance separation from the residual lamp is the art-director's |
| Alarm sweep, venting point sources | A / T | never | n/a | ART 2.8; Alarm Red is rare |

**The falsifier statement carried into the art for the `scr_a` beat (BS-17).** Visually, a FAILED scrubber on a dead bus is indistinguishable from a healthy unpowered one by any T-class pixel. The art therefore **must not** pretend otherwise: no extra wear on `scr_a`'s sprite and no staining to help, since that would be an M-class tell the lie channel could fake. Its falsifiers are SCAN, or waking the bus with the spare breaker and reading the lantern. The only art obligation is that the two lanterns (`scr_a`, `pump_a`) share one sprite and one lit/dark treatment, so a woken bus gives an unambiguous contrast.

### Palette rules

| Element | Palette | Hex (ART 4.1/4.6) | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| Open-end piece body | Graphite mid, lip highlight Graphite lit | `#2A303A` / `#5C6670` | matte, hue-less, no emission, no glow |
| Open-end **medium** | the loop's own hue in FT's trail medium: O2 Cyan mist, Coolant Ice frost, Fuel Ember puddle edge | `#3FD6E8` / `#C8E6F5` / `#F06A28` | the hue belongs to the loop, not to the piece; trails keep full saturation under red light (ART 2.8) |
| Cap piece | Graphite body, **Hull Grey bead 4 px** | `#2A303A`, `#8C949C` | Hull Grey is thin-element only; run the ART 4.4 hue >= 36 degree sample on the bead under every Tungsten source, including cutter sparks |
| Kerf | Void Steel to Graphite mid | `#0B1018` to `#2A303A` | no hue, no glow, no warm tone (warm steel crowds the flesh band, ART 4.1) |
| Loose-yield sprite | Graphite mid/lit body + 2 px non-emissive loop-hue accent (AD-BS-7) | per loop | albedo only; never emissive; never outlined |
| Carried-module sprite | Graphite mid + Graphite lit edges, Void Steel 1 px contour, **no loop hue** | `#2A303A` / `#5C6670` / `#0B1018` | one shared sprite |
| Beam | Tungsten core | `#FFE2A8` | unchanged by carrying, channelling, tell or breach (ART 2.7) |
| Glowstick | Chemlight, world light only | `#86B896` | never UI, never a pipe, fill or fixture; no Chemlight pixel on any UI element (AC-IA-122) |
| Cutter / welder sparks | Tungsten family | `#FFCC66` family | no Fuel Ember pixel, no flesh-band pixel |
| Prompt / bar | Ground `#1C2128`, border Graphite lit, fill Tungsten fixture, needle Paper White | per Interaction | unchanged |
| Alarm | Alarm Red, sweep only | `#E0102A` | never a pickup, drop, cut or cap cue, never text |
| Residual lamp | Tungsten, steady, at MVP (a loop-hue residual is Vertical Slice, DA V-7) | `#FFCC66` | never iridescent, never modulated |
| Sanity | Violet `#8A3FD1` core / `#B07BE6` fringe; Flesh `#D9A58C` / `#B5705C` | n/a | **absent from every MVP sortie visual**; a class (b) gleam on a lying reading is the only violet allowed on the screen layer; the class (d) leak at the loose sprite's fringe is VS-A |

*Loop hues and the open-end pieces.* Hue is redundant with shape (ART 4.0 principle 2; 3.3). The piece names the loop in greyscale: O2 = 6 px round + ring-clamp remnant; coolant = 14 px lagged (10 core + wrap, frayed at the cut) + padded-collar remnant; fuel = 18 px + bolted-flange remnant (plus diagonal hazard banding where the pipe carries it); power and integrity have no open-end piece. The three pieces differ by a 4 px diameter step, so the loop survives 50 percent scale (ART 8.2). *Luminance ladder checks (ART 4.5).* The kerf (Void Steel to Graphite mid, L* about 5 to 20) collides in greyscale with a dried arc scorch (also dark): **shape carries it** (linear versus radial). The cap bead Hull Grey (L* about 61) collides with the Fuel Ember puddle edge (L* about 61): on a capped fuel end the ember edge is gone (the medium ended), so the pair is not co-present on one cap; if a puddle residue sits next to a bead, shape (bead = thin straight line, puddle = glossy flat area) carries it. *The sane-world flesh-band test.* Hull Grey under fixture Tungsten computes to about hue 43 degrees, S 56 percent, V 55 percent (ART 4.4 near-case); the cap bead and any Hull Grey trim on the carried sprite are 4 px thin elements only. **Asset review order for this system: the warm-lit Hull Grey cap bead first, then lit Graphite under cutter sparks, then the Chemlight on Hull Grey (hue about 140 degrees, safest).** The cap bead is added to AC-IA-122's sample list (ASK-BS-AD2).

### Audio existence list (existence and sync only)

Boarding specifies no layers, ducking, fades or triggers. These are the audio classes the sortie's visuals **require to exist**, and the sync each must keep with its visual (owners in parentheses). *Engine note:* the Godot reference `audio.md` informs only this event list (the 4.7 `AudioStreamPlayer2D` `area_mask` default 0, `[UNV]`); the reference tree has no module for the rule composition itself.

| Class that must exist | Sync with the visual | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| Open-end hiss per medium (O2, coolant, fuel), continuous until capped | begins the tick the open end appears; ends the tick the medium ends (`cap_port` or section dormant) | FT, Graph acoustic classes |
| Cut-completion LOUD one-shot | the same tick as the kerf swap | Interaction V8 row |
| Cutter and welder tool loops | start no later than the work loop's first frame | Interaction |
| Cap seat cue | the same tick as the cap piece | Interaction |
| Pickup and set-down cues | the swap frame; none for a dark drop beyond the set-down (no sonar ping for the item) | Interaction |
| Load breath signature by carried class | begins no later than the pickup frame; blind-identifiable (SE RK-2, at least 90 percent) | Suit, audio-director |
| Pod-transit silence and dock hush | transit begins at the press; the hush ends at the arrival settle | audio-director |
| Tell audio component | starts no later than its visual (HR-9) | audio-director |
| Structure-borne tell bed (Attention) | present from tell beat 0 to the release (it sags on abort); non-positional; never a level-mapped cue (AT-26) | Attention (AT-15, AUD-4), audio-director |
| Hum / HUM_DROP of a running machine | true-state audio (LSG:976); corroboration, not the falsifier of record until the Graph rules; the cut of a running machine drops its island's hum | audio-director (C-BS-4) |

*Mood the audio should match (comment only).* Cold, dead, patient (ART 2.6); the hush of the pod is a held breath, not a score. **No musical sting for a cut, a pickup, an install or a retreat.**

Counts: 12 pipe-end slices, 2 pedestal states, 5 carried frames, 5 anchor slices, about 18 loose-yield sprites, plus the character frames. No new category token; one new anchor name; one state-token reuse (`cut` on a pipe end).

### Review checks (evidence rows are AC-BS-61, AC-BS-62, AC-BS-97 to AC-BS-100)

| Check | Kind | Statement |
|---|---|---|
| R-BS-1 | lint (import) | In every carry, pickup, drop and stagger frame at all five facings: no module pixel within 3 px of `anchor_chest_<facing>` and the first 12 px of the beam axis ray is clear of module pixels. Fails the asset on any frame |
| R-BS-2 | lint (import) | The carry cycle's ground contact has zero foot slip at 60 px/s (`V_CARRY`) |
| R-BS-3 | manual | At 25 percent scale, no colour, no contour, the carrying figure reads helmet circle + pack rectangle + a block breaking the outline upward, and the module does not merge into the helmet circle |
| R-BS-4 | manual (greyscale) | A kerf, an authored arc scorch and a coolant frost fan are told apart at 50 percent scale in greyscale |
| R-BS-5 | manual | Hue-removed, the three open-end pieces name their loop by diameter and fitting remnant |
| R-BS-6 | manual | Sampled hex lists: no Fuel Ember or flesh-band pixel in cutter or welder sparks; the cap bead and lit Hull Grey under every Tungsten source stay at hue >= 36 degrees; no Chemlight pixel on any UI element |
| R-BS-7 | manual (dark scene) | A module dropped in the dark is not visible and shows no marker, ghost or outline until lit; no glowstick or pool persists as a waypoint after it wanes |
| R-BS-8 | manual | Across a healthy and a FAILED node, the cut bar, work loop, spark cadence and completion frames are identical; loose-yield sprites carry no condition variants; the kerf does not show how much was cut before an abort |
| R-BS-9 | manual (drift stages 1 to 3; stage 3 is VS-A) | Prompt, bar, carried sprite and beam are unchanged; module sprites, gauges and (VS-A) loose sprites show their leaks at edges only |
| R-BS-10 | manual | During a tell with a module in hand, beam radius, colour and direction are unchanged and the carry pose does not change; only the cone rim differs (ART 2.7) |
| R-BS-11 | manual | No sortie frame, at any accessibility setting, contains a value tag, rating, rarity colour, loot glow, condition bar, pack, weight or encumbrance readout, listen or Attention indicator, sortie timer or autosave toast |
| R-BS-12 | manual | At the maximum module carry the figure plus module is at most 71 px and never overlaps the wall cap layer when walking along a wall |

### Flags and routes

| ID | Item | Route |
|---|---|---|
| F-1 | **C-BS-5 ruled:** overhead carry, 24 x 14 cap (AD-BS-1, AD-BS-2). The PC-8 phrase "below the lamp" is refreshed to "clear of the chest point and the forward beam arc; overhead at MVP" | Controller wording refresh (refresh row 19) |
| F-2 | **Kerf versus power-arc scorch** (AD-BS-3): the kerf is linear and the arc scorch is radial; ART 3.3 should note the kerf as the sixth, non-loop mark so no future artist makes it radial | art bible maintenance (ASK-BS-AD2) |
| F-3 | **New anchor name `anchor_carry_<facing>`** (the ART 8.5 anchor list has chest, hand, pack, helmet, limb) | ART 8.5 maintenance (ASK-BS-AD2), technical-artist |
| F-4 | **State token `cut` extended from doors to pipe ends**; no new `stub` token | ART 8.5 one-line maintenance (ASK-BS-AD2) |
| F-5 | **The loop-hue accent on a dead loose module is albedo, not emission** (AD-BS-7); ART 4.2 does not say which | art bible clarification (ASK-BS-AD2) |
| F-6 | **Can a phantom enter CARRIED?** If yes, the shared carried sprite (an anchor) cannot leak and the ladder (weight, SCAN, the trip home) is the only falsifier; if no, a phantom is un-pickable and answers like an unlit object (BS-15, EM-21, IA-13). The art holds in both branches | Sanity, Interaction, Perception Filter (OQ-BS-13) |
| F-7 | **Proposed visual correlate of the load signature** (deeper, slower pack rise under HEAVY): not adopted | Suit and audio-director; passes SE RK-2 or is dropped |
| F-8 | **The `pod_hop` hop-availability mechanism (BS-19), ruled:** the docked pod's self-powered status glow, an ordinary lit fixture; the IA-6 refusal stands; the console must not become an unowned self-lit prop; the luminance separation from the visible-unknown lamp is owed by the art-director *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | art-director, Emitter State, Derelict Authoring, Web Map (OQ-BS-4) |
| F-9 | **Optional VS settle on the yield** is the only eased motion proposed; confirm with the technical-artist that a 4 px no-bounce drop does not conflict with the Y-sort of a loose item at the node anchor | technical-artist |
| F-10 | **Tool-swap duration unregistered** (`T_EQUIP`, SN OQ-SN-6): the equip frames' length is not specified; any figure is a lower bound | Suit, Sanity (ASK-BS-N1) |
| F-11 | **Open-end piece placement** follows the `anchor_port_<n>` slices on the pedestal sheet; Layout's port geometry is not yet published for the MVP derelicts | Layout, DA (ASK-BS-D1) |
| F-12 | **A stowed hull-plate stack is not drawn**; its only cue is the breath. If playtest shows players cannot tell they hold a stowed plate, the answer is the Suit's cue and never a HUD icon (G-5) | Suit |
| F-13 | **Hum versus the closed list** (C-BS-4): art treats hum as true-state corroboration, not the falsifier of record until the Graph rules (LSG:976, C-BS-4); no visual correlate of hum exists | Graph (ASK-BS-G1), audio-director |
| F-14 | **Scale lie accepted:** the shared carried sprite (at most 24 x 14) is much smaller than a 96 x 96 machine; accepted as abstraction of load (BS-22) and confirmed against the 25 percent test | creative-director, if playtest reads it as inconsistent |

**Truth and pillar summary.** P1: every price channel (hiss, mist, frost, breath, pace, noise) is drawn from the T or A classes and nothing in the sortie shows a value or a condition. P3: nothing glows for the player's benefit (no afterglow on the kerf, no glowing loot, no self-lit console, no persistent marker; the one exception is the docked pod's own status glow, an owned pod fixture costed in D.2 that carries the way home, BS-19); a lit job is a visible job. P4: a lie can live only in M-class pixels and always leaks at an edge; anchors never shift. P2: the retreat image is the trail outrunning a carrier and the alarm sweep; no art ever pins the player. P5: the pedestal and kerf are the permanent, readable record of what was taken.

After the art bible is approved, run `/asset-spec system:boarding-and-salvage` to produce per-asset visual descriptions, dimensions and generation prompts from this section (the open-end and cap pieces, the pedestal-with-kerf state and the carried-module sprite first).

## Game Feel

Boarding & Salvage has no input, no frame and no animation of its own (BS-1), so its feel is the feel of **what the composition makes the owners' systems do in sequence**: how long the player is made to stand still before a take, how soon the world answers a cut, how slow and how loud the way home is, and how legal it is to put the prize down. Every number below is either cited from its owner (never restated as ours) or derived from an owner's value by a Formulas entry here. **Units:** 1 tick = 16.7 ms (`TICK_RATE` 60); a tile is `TILE_PX` 48 px; speeds are `V_WALK` 2, `V_RUN` 3 and `V_CARRY` 1 px per tick (24, 16 and 48 ticks per tile). Every sortie duration is a **lower bound** while the tool-swap time is unregistered (`T_EQUIP`, SN OQ-SN-6). Placeholders carry the owner's tag. The target emotion is **a held breath, then a heavy walk**: slow, deliberate, exposed, with one door out that is always open.

### Feel reference

**Borrow:** *Project Zomboid*'s carried burden that changes how you move and its timed contextual actions (a linear bar, cancelled by moving, no skill check); the hands-free chest lamp of *Pandorum* (the beam stays on the job while both hands work and while they are full); *Lethal Company*'s run back with hands full and the real story of dropping the scrap to survive; *Alien: Isolation*'s stillness as a strategy. **Anti-reference:** a numeric weight readout or mass-scaled speed (Zomboid's moodle), a repair minigame or quick-time event (a UI surface that could leak module-body truth and a rhythm demand the "I can leave now" test forbids), a motion tracker (an Attention meter in another form, AT-20), and any treasure framing (a pickup glow, a reward chime, a sting).

### Input-to-feedback chain (the sortie, in order)

| # | Player act | What the player sees and hears | Latency or duration | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Press the console (`pod_hop`), hands free or full | keycap drop and press cue at the Applied result; the black window; the avatar and cargo are not drawn | ack in the same render frame, Applied at most 1 tick later; the leg and settle are **945 ticks (15.75 s)** at Web Map's [PLACEHOLDER] figures | Interaction, Web Map |
| 2 | Arrive | the dark room, the beam, one residual lamp, dust; the pod hush ends at the settle | the actor is IDLE at once; the prompt shows after the HUD debounce `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` 13 (217 ms, HUD-owned, registered) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* | Web Map, HUD |
| 3 | Stand still (listen) | hiss and creak where lit, Attention's occasional floor-gated hull creaks (AT-26), pack breath, dust in the cone; **nothing else** | idle pushes 0 noise (PC-10); the Attention excess decays on its own constant (`TAU_ATT_TICKS` 1800 [PLACEHOLDER], 30 s) | Controller, Attention |
| 4 | Walk to the node | the beam sweeps; the prompt appears only for a lit, focused node | 24 ticks per tile, 24 AU per tile | Controller |
| 5 | Press and hold `interact` on the node (`begin_cut`) | keycap drop and the first work pose at once; the bar appears at fill 0; sparks at the grip | ack at most 1 frame; Applied at most 1 tick; the first effect of the channel at `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6 ticks; the bar then fills in stepped px | Interaction, HUD |
| 6 | Keep holding (the cut) | a linear bar with a needle-mark, the 6-frame work loop, TOOL-class tool sound; the module stays on its mount | `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 [PLACEHOLDER] = **4 s**; the LONG class bar of 40 px interior is 6 ticks per art px | Interaction |
| 7 | The cut completes | in **one frame**: the module becomes an empty pedestal with a linear kerf; the yield sits on the deck; open-end media start; the LOUD one-shot sounds; work loop and sparks stop; the bar holds full for `BAR_HOLD_DONE_TICKS` 12 then cuts | Applied inside the tick the channel completes; `U_LOUD` 180 [PLACEHOLDER] is pushed on that tick; no success sting | Interaction, Attention |
| 8 | Release or move mid-cut (abort) | the fill snaps empty, the break-notch holds 12 ticks, the module is untouched, no half-kerf | the move wins within 1 tick (PC-7); the partial is kept for `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600 [PLACEHOLDER] (10 s) | Interaction |
| 9 | Optional: swap to the welder and cap (`begin_cap`) | the stub's hiss and medium stop on the same tick as the cap piece appears | `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS` 90 per stub = 1.5 s, plus an unregistered swap | Interaction |
| 10 | Tap `interact` to pick the yield up | the loose sprite is replaced by the raised-hold carry sprite **in the same frame**; the prompt becomes `G . Drop`; the breath takes the load signature | depress at the press, click at the Applied result (1 tick); 2 to 3 frames of lift; the breath no later than the pickup frame | Interaction, Suit |
| 11 | Walk home carrying | a third of a run: **48 ticks per tile**, no helmet bounce, no foot slip; the beam stays on; a trail or the dark leans in | `V_CARRY` 1 px per tick (60 px/s, 1.25 tiles/s); 48 AU of locomotion noise per tile whatever the mass (IDB F-13), plus 336 AU of burden per tile while salvage is held aboard the wreck (`U_BURDEN` 7 x 48, Attention F-26) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* | Controller, Suit |
| 12 | A tell opens | only the cone rim changes; the audio component starts no later than the visual | a played tell is `TELL_DRAW_LO` 900 [PLACEHOLDER] to `TELL_DRAW_HI` 1800 [PLACEHOLDER] ticks (15 to 30 s); in the shortest, a carry covers at most 18 tiles; under the ruled carry burden it opens 318 ticks (5.3 s) after the pickup, inside the walk (D.2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* | Attention, Lighting |
| 13 | Press `drop` | the arms lower in 2 to 3 frames, the module sits at the placement cell, the pace jumps to `V_RUN` (16 ticks per tile, 56 tiles in 900 ticks) | free, instant, unlit-legal; the next TAP waits `TAP_REARM_TICKS` 12 | Interaction, Controller |
| 14 | Meet a closed door while carrying | the door's ordinary plate *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | the press costs `DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` 30 [PLACEHOLDER] | Interaction |
| 15 | Press the console with the module in the arms | the same press as step 1; the module is not drawn in transit | the hop is accepted in a TELL or BREACH (WM-26) | Web Map |
| 16 | Arrive home, walk to the hardpoint, tap `install_module` | the carried sprite hands over to the seated machine in one frame; the hardpoint arms | a TAP: 1 tick; the install chain runs inside one tick | Assembly |

So the answer to "I started a cut" is a bar and a work pose within one frame, a module that **does not leave its mount for four seconds**, and then, in one frame, a pedestal, a pile and a bang. The answer to "I lifted it" is a changed breath and a prompt that has lost every verb but one. Nothing in this chain is gated on a Boarding tick, because Boarding has none.

### Weight (the carry is the felt cost)

- **The ratio is the feel.** Carry : walk : run is **1 : 2 : 3** in px per tick and **3 : 2 : 1** in ticks per tile (48, 24, 16). A 13-tile frame is crossed in 5.2 s walking and 10.4 s carrying. Noise per tile is **24 AU walking, 48 AU carrying, 64 AU running** (`NOISE_PER_TICK` walk 1, carry 1, run 4 [PLACEHOLDER], D.1 and D.2; locomotion only, and a salvage carry aboard the wreck adds 336 AU of burden per tile, D.2 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*): carrying is the slowest *and* louder-per-tile than walking, and running is the loudest. Because speed never changes with mass, the 6 kg core and the 90 kg cell feel identical in pace; the difference is the breath (SE-28) and nothing else.
- **No ramp, no slide.** Carry starts and stops instantly like the walk (PCI Game Feel); there is no acceleration curve to read as weight. The weight is the *constant* low speed.
- **Hands full is a verb loss.** On pickup every prompt in the world vanishes except `G . Drop` and, at a fitting hardpoint, the console or a door face, its one press (BS-9) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. The player feels the carry as a narrowing of what the world offers, not as a number.
- **The pace is the lesson.** A trail, a draught or an alarm sweep that outruns a carrier teaches retreat without text (ART 2.8): at `V_CARRY` the web's propagation is faster than the player; at `V_RUN` after a `drop` it is not.
- **The body tells the truth.** Stagger is an input lock and an animation, never a shove; a carry stagger keeps the module; the walk-only cap at CRITICAL vitals is read off the visor fog and the breath (PCI), never a banner.

### Snap targets (what is one frame, and what is never eased)

| Moment | Target | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Module to pedestal-with-kerf | one frame | a cut, not a dissolve (ART 7.3 spirit) |
| Loose sprite to carried sprite | same frame | no fly-to-inventory; no inventory exists |
| Carried sprite to seated machine | one frame | the install is a seat, not a flourish |
| Cap piece appears and the medium ends | same tick | the consequence ends when the act ends (P1) |
| Open-end medium starts | the tick the cut completes | the price is visible at once |
| Work loop and sparks stop | the tick the cut completes | no recoil or overshoot (IA recovery 0) |
| Yield settle | none at MVP; VS optional at most 6 ticks, a 4 px drop, no bounce | no eased motion near a salvage node (G-5) |
| Prompt hide | the same render frame the target is lost or unlit | a prompt for an unlit object would lie |
| Rejection | a cut and a dull cue; no shake, flash or red | rejections never decorate (IA V-10) |
| Full-frame effects on cut, hop, breach, pickup | none | HR-13 and the no-flash rule |

### The three rhythms of a sortie

1. **Boarding rhythm (ART 2.6).** Cold, dead, patient. The player is made to stand still before they are allowed to act: the arrival room offers only the beam, dust, a dried trail and the hull's creaks, and every action after it costs light. Target dwell before the first cut: the player chooses; the design target is that **a player who pauses 2 s or more before the first cut is typical**, not forced (X-BS-2). Nothing moves unless the player moves it.
2. **The tell (ART 2.7).** Beam, ambient, radius, temperature and direction do not change; only the rim of the cone gets busier. The tell is **10 to 30 s** by Pillar 2 and in play **15 to 30 s** (`TELL_DRAW_LO` 900 to `TELL_DRAW_HI` 1800 [PLACEHOLDER]); a carry begun at tell entry covers at most 18 tiles (`tell_window_fit`, D.3), so a carry still ahead of more than 18 tiles when the tell opens cannot beat the shortest tell; under the ruled shape the tell opens 318 ticks into a carry of about 21 to 28 tiles, so the *decision* "keep walking with it, or set it down" is live in kind and thin in margin (140 permille of the draw at 28 tiles; the pass line is the boarding spike's, AC-BS-132) (D.2, D.3, **OQ-BS-27**) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. A cut begun at tell entry completes (240 ticks) and leaves 660 ticks, 13 tiles.
3. **The breach (ART 2.8).** Controlled panic. Alarm Red sweeps for 2 to 3 s, a failure trail runs along a graph edge faster than a carrier walks, and the answer the world teaches is *drop it and go*. `drop` is free; the run at `V_RUN` 3 is three times the carry; the console is one press; the hop is always allowed. There is no forced drop and no refusal (BS-13).

### The emotional arc of the reference sortie (design-time, not a meter)

Mapped to BS-2's steps and the art bible's rhythms. Times are illustrative targets for the reference sortie (`htr_a` to `hp_cargo_heater`), not lints.

| Step | Target emotion | Art state | Flow note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pod leg out | smallness, anticipation | ART 2.5 | the only exterior; an intentional flow break |
| Arrive, stand still | forensic curiosity under a held breath | ART 2.6 | flow entry: low challenge, high attention |
| Read and decide | curiosity turning to appetite | ART 2.6 / 6.5 | competence: reading witnesses |
| Cut (4 s) | commitment; a small guilt at the noise | ART 2.6 to 2.7 edge | the peak of agency |
| Cap or leave | conscientiousness versus haste | ART 2.6 | optional and priced (BS-8) |
| Lift and carry | exposure, burden, ownership | ART 2.7 if Attention climbed | challenge rises; the drop decision is live |
| Tell during the carry | "it noticed", then keep or drop | ART 2.7 | P2: 10 to 30 s; the legal exits (BS-13) |
| Breach during the carry | controlled panic | ART 2.8 | drop and run at `V_RUN` 3 against `V_CARRY` 1 |
| Console press, hop home | relief; smallness again | ART 2.5 | a flow break |
| Install | ownership; then the new cost | ART 2.3 | the prize has a consequence at home (PWR-OVL arms, DA:855) |

### Pacing (design-time intensity, 0 calm to 10 peak; not a metric and not a meter)

Reference sortie, line D (`htr_a`), the floor case with ticks from arrival (routes are the assumed anchors of D.1, ASK-BS-D1):

| Segment | Ticks (floor) | Intensity | What carries it | Rest or release |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hop out (ART 2.5) | 945 | 2 | hands free; the residual lamp as a point | rest |
| Arrive and listen (ART 2.6) | 0 to 600 | 2 to 3 | a black room, dust, the beam only | **rest valley** |
| Dock to spine, door 1 | 54 + 30 | 3 | a dried frost fan at the elbow | - |
| The spine (frost sill and lamp sight lines) | 144 | 4 | the decision junction (warm east, cold south) | - |
| Door 2 open and the cold walk-in | 30 + 102 | 6 to 7 | the cold, the breath, the frost line | - |
| **The cut** (LOUD at the end) | 240 | 8 to 9 | the bar fills linearly; a lit job; the LOUD one-shot | **peak** |
| Carry out, before the tell *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* | 318 of the ruled 1008 to 1344 (at the 13-tile anchor, 318 of 624) | 6 rising to 7 | slow, loaded; out of the cold, the rim begins to stir | building |
| **The tell and the decision** (keep walking, or set it down) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* | the rest of the carry: 690 to 1,026 on the ruled route (306 at the anchor) | 8 to 9 | the rim busies (ART 2.7); the dock is not yet in sight on the ruled route | **second peak**; release at the console press or at the drop |
| Dock, console press | about 30 | 4 | the dock hush | release |
| Hop back | 945 | 1 to 2 | hands full but safe; no burden in the pod (EC-BS-63) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* | **rest valley** |
| Home carry and install | about 500 | 3 rising to 5 | the heater arms PWR-OVL, a new home clock the player caused | tension handed to the home loop |

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Pre-ruling history (to the stage-12 revision below, these sentences describe the sortie without the burden term):** Attention's level along this line rises through the sortie. Its noise term alone peaks at about 29 percent of `ATT_THRESHOLD`; the **full lit push** (noise, the chest beam and the lit idle home ship; D.2 restated with `I_light` and `I_power`) peaks at 57 percent, and at the placeholders neither opens a tell. At the placeholders the felt intensity is therefore carried by the cold, the light, the loaded gait and the hum, **not by the integrator**. *Stage-12 revision (CD-GDD-ALIGN concern):* that is the state of the placeholders, not an acceptable MVP state. The routed thread is "carry it home under rising Attention" and the MVP kill/continue gate tests a boarding-to-breach two-rhythm loop; a reference sortie that can finish without any tell tests the first rhythm only. **The design target is X-BS-11: a reference sortie played with the beam on and normal dwell meets at least one tell in at least 3 of 5 playtest sorties (re-based under the ruled carry-state intake: a lit carry meets a tell by construction, so restraint stays a real strategy through the outcome, with 1 to 4 of 5 of the sorties that meet a tell ending in a drop, an abort or a breach, X-BS-11) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.** The pacing table above stays the target for the *human* intensity (the cold, the clock); it is not a substitute for the web answering. Meeting X-BS-11 is a three-owner ruling and not Attention's lever alone (OQ-BS-27, ruled by the user as option B on 2026-10-27 and a precondition of the MVP playtest; D.2 shows it cannot be met by `U_TOOL`, `U_LOUD` and `TAU_ATT_TICKS` alone, nor by a retune that does not make the tell the take's own doing) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the "weigh the noise" decision for a single cut (X-BS-9) rides on the same ruling. A tell met during the carry is where the table's "sustained, slow release" becomes "rising". *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Under the ruled carry-state intake the lit line opens a tell inside the carry (D.2), and the carry row of the table stretches to the ruled 1008 to 1344 ticks of the decision route (D.1); the intensity is 'rising' from the tell's opening. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* The table now splits the carry at the tell's opening (318 ticks at the placeholders) into a build segment and a decision segment.

### Anti-targets (feel the system must not produce)

A reward chime, sparkle or glow on a take; an inventory or weight readout; a sortie timer; an Attention meter in any channel; a forced drop or a hop refusal; a speed buff, stamina bar or adrenaline state in a tell or breach (rhythm independence: identical input, vitals and load give identical motion in every state); a QTE or minigame in the cut; a stinger on any beat; an autosave toast mid-carry; a hit-stop, shake or flash (Derelict Authoring authors none either).

### Playtest-checkable targets

Format and protocol follow Derelict Authoring's: five testers, at least three outside the project (the concept's kill/continue gate). **Creative-director ruling (CD-GDD-ALIGN, stage 12): the thresholds below are adopted as written as the provisional MVP thresholds, tagged `[PROVISIONAL: creative-director]`.** They are the pass lines of the MVP playtest (five testers, at least three outside the project) and stay provisional until a second round of eight or more testers promotes them (OQ-BS-20); five testers and 4-of-5 thresholds are a smoke signal, not a statistic, and a miss is a finding routed as in "If a target fails", never a waiver. **X-BS-3 (the `scr_a` falsifier) remains the condition that decides whether the `scr_a` beat survives (D-CD-3 stands).** **Observer protocol:** a response counts when the observer, using a pre-agreed phrase list, records a spoken reference to the named concept within 10 s of the trigger; two observers code independently and a disagreement counts as not met. Each target becomes a manual acceptance row (AC-BS-69 to AC-BS-78, AC-BS-122 for X-BS-11, and AC-BS-124 to AC-BS-126 for X-BS-12 to X-BS-14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

|---|---|---|

The upper bound is 5 min and not 6 because the median must stay below the sanity stage-1 point (315.8 s, D.8); an 8 min median (480 s) equals `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` and is well past it. The windows are D.1's. A long sortie that enters stage 1 is intended (Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20), D73 part (c)); the band concerns the median only. Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20) (D73): the concept's five-minute W_LOOP is kept and content is budgeted for about 200 s of discretionary dwell *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **One target** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: W_LOOP 300 s is the single target; the design-expectation median W_ABOARD derived from it is about 259 s (lint-guaranteed 229 s, OQ-BS-28 (d)) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, and the band's 5-minute top is the stage-1 ceiling on the median, not a second target (BS-20) | AC-BS-69 |

The deterministic margins live in one generated file, `REF_GOLDEN` (D.2): the lit run's tell opens inside the carry and the level reaches `L_CEIL` before the console *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*, and the no-carry variants (cut and leave; listen and leave) stay at or below 0.95 x `theta_HOLD`, so the manual rows measure players and not the arithmetic. **Ruled (OQ-BS-27, option B); a precondition of the MVP playtest:** with Attention's recalibration run (F-26) the tell opens 318 ticks into the carry and the live share is 140 permille of the draw at 28 tiles and none at 21 to 24, so the MVP kill/continue playtest does not run until `REF_GOLDEN` passes AC-BS-123 with the boarding spike's pass line set (AC-BS-132) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*. **Decision quality** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: an outcome band cannot tell a live choice from a dominated one, so the row also requires that at least 3 of 5 testers who meet a tell while carrying state a reason before choosing (think-aloud, recorded by the observer before the outcome: the dock's distance, the noise, the time left as they read it), while the deterministic `REF_GOLDEN` variants (D.2) carry the margin | AC-BS-122, AC-BS-123 |

| X-BS-14 | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **First-door choice and leading elements.** At the `a_spine` junction the single leading element of A pulls toward `scr_a`, away from the reference payoff: the observer records which door each tester opens first; at least 2 of 5 [PROVISIONAL: creative-director] reach `a_machine` without being told. This is a finding row for the leading-elements and sightline pass with Derelict Authoring (cross-GDD note), never a reason to add a marker or a hint (G-5, G-7) | AC-BS-126 |

*If a target fails:* X-BS-1 high means the route or node placement (Derelict Authoring), never `V_CARRY`; X-BS-7 failing as "boring" means the carry is too long for its tension and the levers are route length, node placement and Attention's placeholders, never a new pressure that the world does not show; X-BS-9 failing means OQ-BS-27 and OQ-BS-3; X-BS-11 failing low (too few sorties meet a tell) means the ruling of OQ-BS-27 (option B as ruled: Attention's carry burden `U_BURDEN`, the boarding spike's pass line, and the length of Derelict Authoring's reference decision carry *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), never a new pressure the world does not show, and failing high means the same levers pulled too far; X-BS-12 failing means the door cue needs a stronger diegetic form (never text), X-BS-13 failing means the frost, fog or breath cue is too faint (Derelict Authoring and Suit), and X-BS-14 failing is a finding for the sightline pass. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

## UI Requirements

Boarding & Salvage has **no screen, widget or menu of its own** (BS-1, BS-24). It has UI *content*: the composition rules of the sortie decide which of the HUD's existing surfaces are allowed to show what, and, more often, what they must leave empty. Every surface below is owned by another system (Interaction's prompt plate and bar, the HUD & Suit Layer's hand rail and suit panel, Web Map's console and Chart Table, Save/Load's single cue); Boarding states the content constraints that the UX pass must honour. The first constraint is the one that defines the whole section: **the sortie's UI is mostly what is absent** (G-5, G-6, R-BS-11).

### Surfaces the sortie touches

| Surface | Owner | What the sortie needs it to show | What the sortie forbids it to show | Evidence row |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Prompt plate: the carry state** | Interaction (IA V-1, V-13), HUD & Suit Layer | While carrying, exactly the prompts of BS-9's closed set: `G . Drop` at the foot origin always; the install plate (`verb_install`) at a HARDPOINT whose `compatible_defs` lists the carried def; the hop plate at the POD_CONSOLE; nothing at a DOCK_CONSOLE; the door's ordinary V4 plate at a door face *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Nothing on any other world target. The plate's key-cap follows the active device's grammar (ART 7.2) and the player's rebinding; `G` is the default keyboard binding | A prompt for any other target; a "hands full" or "cannot" message; a greyed-out verb; a different plate for a FAILED or incompatible hardpoint (the absence of a plate is the whole answer, and it is identical for a healthy and a FAILED carried instance, IA-11) | AC-BS-25, AC-BS-97 |
| **Prompt plate: the cut, cap and scan** | Interaction (IA V-1, V-3) | The state-neutral verb (`verb_cut` for the cut, `verb_weld` for the cap, since it is the welder's ordinary verb per IA:270, and the scanner's verb for the scan; the keys are Interaction's) on a lit, focused node; the plate appears and vanishes at the beam rim with the object | A module name, a condition, a value, a rating, a mass, a stub count, a "worth it" hint | AC-BS-12, AC-BS-98 |
| **The linear channel bar** | Interaction (IA V-4, V-5, V-7), HUD & Suit Layer | A stepped linear bar with a needle-mark: the LONG class for the cut (`SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 [PLACEHOLDER], 40 px interior, 6 ticks per art px), the MEDIUM class for the cap (`CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS`) and the scan (`FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` 120 [PLACEHOLDER]); abort snaps to empty with the break-notch held 12 ticks; the module stays on its mount while the bar fills | A ring (the kernel text's "progress ring" is corrected to a bar, ART 7.3); a bar that differs by the subject's `hp`; a half-cut mark on the module; a percentage; a timer | AC-BS-98 |
| **The hand rail** | HUD & Suit Layer | The tool glyphs for slots 1 to 3 (cutter, welder, scanner; the suppressant is removed from the MVP kit, user ruling X6 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*) with the bound tool in the Tungsten fixture colour; the **carry state** (the raised-hold glyph or the hands-full indication, drawn in the rail's grammar and never as a weight); the glowstick count; the beam toggle | A weight number, a capacity count, a pack grid, a "slots free" count, a mass bar, an encumbrance moodle | AC-BS-97 |
| **The suit panel** | HUD & Suit Layer, Suit | `CELL_S` [PLACEHOLDER] and `RESERVE_S` [PLACEHOLDER] as the Suit defines them (the sortie's physical budget); the body cue is breath and visor fog in the world, not a gauge | A sortie timer, a "time aboard" counter, a stage-of-sanity indicator, an Attention or noise indicator of any kind (the one-bit `suit.alarm` mark, user ruling X9, is a suit-integrity bit and not an Attention indicator) | AC-BS-65, R-BS-11 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| **The pod console and the Chart Table** | Web Map & Transit, Interaction | The console is in sight on arrival (BS-3); the hop is one press, with cargo; the Chart Table shows what the crew lit (no fuel, time or cost beside a selection: WM-35, AB-5) | A cargo manifest, "module secured", a weight or fuel readout at the console, a remaining-salvage count, a loot value (ART 7.4) | AC-BS-30, AC-BS-47 |
| **The autosave cue** | Save/Load (SL-4) | Save/Load's own single non-diegetic cue in the chart-table register, if it shows one at all | A toast, a corner icon or a spinner during a sortie; any change to the carry pose or walk cycle when a save lands mid-carry | R-BS-11 |
| **Field-check reading** | Interaction, Sanity; drawn by HUD & Suit Layer (HS-33; In-World Instruments K-9) | A reading in the ART 3.4 register (Paper White numerals) that ticks honestly in the MVP; at VS-A a lying reading carries the HR-5 tri-modal tell | A verdict icon, a green check, a red cross, an "OK" or "FAILED" word | AC-BS-39 |

### Accessibility notes (routed to the project-wide requirements)

- **Redundant channels.** The identity of a loop at an open end is redundant with shape (diameter and fitting remnant), not hue alone (ART 4.0 principle 2; R-BS-5); the carry state is redundant between the hand-rail glyph, the raised-hold sprite and the prompt set.
- **The load cue is audio-first, and the ruled package answers it in part** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The Suit's weight cue is breath with a load signature (SE-28), and its blind-identification test is audio (SE RK-2). The user accepted the Suit accessibility package (X10, D44): the `cap.suit.*` caption ids; the rule that haptics driven only by the player's own true body cues are not Attention indicators (added to Adaptive Audio AA-42); and the exceptions E1 (stowed MEDIUM load) and E2 (heat STRAINED to CRITICAL). A deaf or muted player in the dark also gets an opt-in haptic pulse on the tell's beats that carries the same information as the audio (D51). The visual correlate of the load signature (a deeper, slower pack rise under HEAVY, Visual/Audio F-7) stays **not adopted**. The accessibility tier is *standard*, and `design/accessibility-requirements.md` is to be authored later with `/ux-design` accessibility (noted here, not created). OQ-BS-21 is resolved to this package; whether the load breath has a caption id is Suit's.
- **Photosensitivity and reduced motion.** Cutter and welder sparks and the Chemlight pool obey the 3 Hz ceiling by construction; reduced motion never suppresses open-end media or failure trails (they are the truth channel), slows dust, leaves the carry cycle unchanged and turns the Alarm sweep into a static mark plus sound (HR-8, ART 7.3); reduce flashing defaults ON at first launch (user ruling X10, D79) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
- **Hold versus toggle.** The cut, cap and scan are channels, hold by default with the Controller's toggle option; leaving is always free (release or move).
- **Remapping.** `drop`, `toggle_beam`, the equip slots and `interact` are rebindable per the Controller's PC-2 table; the sortie's prompts render the active binding.

### What this system hands to the UX pass

Three entries in the UX specs must cover Boarding's content, and none is a new screen: (1) `design/ux/hud.md`: the carry-state prompt set (the closed four-verb set, `drop`, `install_module`, `pod_hop` and the door's `set_opening`, and the blank on every other target) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, the channel bar for the cut, cap and scan, the hand-rail carry state, and the absence list above; (2) the in-world pattern library: the open-end piece, the cap piece, the kerf and the shared carried sprite as in-world affordances that carry no text; (3) the Chart Table spec: no remaining-salvage count and no loot value. The UX pass runs through Interaction's and the HUD & Suit Layer's specs, not through a Boarding screen.

📌 **UX Flag — Boarding & Salvage**: this system has UI content but no screen of its own. In Pre-Production, the specs that must cover it are `design/ux/hud.md` (the carry-state prompt set of BS-9 and the blank on every other target, the LONG and MEDIUM channel bars for the cut, cap and scan, the hand-rail carry state and glowstick count, and the explicit absence list: no weight, capacity, value, condition, timer or Attention readout) and the in-world pattern library (the open-end piece, cap piece, kerf and shared carried-module sprite as text-free affordances), reached through the Interaction and HUD & Suit Layer UX passes. Run `/ux-design` for those before writing epics; stories that reference this UI should cite `design/ux/hud.md`, not this GDD directly. Retained screenshots of the carry-state prompt set, the three channel bars and the in-world pieces belong in `production/qa/evidence/` (AC-BS-97 to AC-BS-99).

## Cross-References

Four tables: (1) the conflict and gap register, (2) the wording-refresh table owed to neighbours, (3) the asks, (4) the source-of-truth map from this GDD's references to other GDD sections and registry names. **Nothing in a neighbour's file or in `design/registry/entities.yaml` is edited by this GDD**; refreshes and asks are routed for their owners, and the registry stage converts `referenced_by` from the final text (not from specialist drafts). Kinds: CONFLICT (a locked fact or ruling is departed from or two locked texts disagree), GAP (a needed fact exists in no owner), WORDING (a neighbour's text names Boarding wrongly), RISK (a placeholder or content consequence to watch).

Still no constant is minted. No constant is minted (BS-1).

**Registry conformance (2026-10-28)** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: `hazard_dwell_budget` and `lamp_cell_budget` are conformed to D.4 and D.10 as rewritten on 2026-10-28 (arrival-reserve chain with the G5 guard, 63-240 range, 582 / 266 / 342 / 2262, 6 ticks per lit run, OQ-BS-32 closed); `sortie_dwell_envelope` output text is conformed to the OQ-BS-28 (d) correction (lint-guaranteed W_ABOARD about 229 s, design expectation about 259 s).

**Registry delta (done).** This GDD is now in `referenced_by` for the five formerly pending entries (`mvp_template_roster`, `mvp_fault_allocation`, `mvp_part_ledger`, `salvage_yield`, `STOWED_CUE_FLOOR_CLASS`) and for each other entry the final text actually cites by name (the rows above). **No new constant, no new state block, no new hook, no new mutator** — and no entry for `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `DROP_OFFSET_PX` or `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`, which are owners' constants (`OPEN_END_RATE_PM` was since registered by its owner, the Graph, 2026-10-27; Boarding is only in its `referenced_by`).

## Acceptance Criteria

*Conventions.* GIVEN / WHEN / THEN. Tags: **(auto)** automated and deterministic; **(lint)** a static check; **(review)** read by a human against the text; **(manual)** a playtest with people; **(spike)** a time-boxed technical or design probe; **(UI)** or **(visual)** a retained screenshot in `production/qa/evidence/` (plus lead sign-off for visual); **(advisory)** measured and reported, not asserted. Every row ends in the rule or formula it proves: `[BS-n]`, `[D.n]` or `[Cross-References]`. Rows marked **blocked** wait on a named ask or owner answer and are not waived; rows marked **conditional** apply only if a named ask is refused; rows marked **deferred** wait on a Vertical Slice system and carry that blocker. Rows tagged **BLOCKING** are gates on the implementation of this system: it is not implementation-complete, and the slice does not ship, while any BLOCKING row is red, and no sign-off waives one (AC-BS-25, AC-BS-92 and AC-BS-51b; AC-BS-51a is deleted by the OQ-BS-4 ruling) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Rows described as **a precondition of the MVP playtest** (AC-BS-77, AC-BS-122, AC-BS-123) gate the playtest and not the implementation. Row ids are stable: the numbers are not in file order because the qa-lead's added rows (AC-BS-81 to AC-BS-121) sit with the group they belong to, and AC-BS-122 was added at stage 12; AC-BS-123 to AC-BS-126 were added by the 2026-10-27 design review, AC-BS-127 to AC-BS-131 by the user rulings of the same day, and AC-BS-26, AC-BS-105 and AC-BS-121 were deleted by the review and AC-BS-51a by the ruling and their ids are not reused *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

*Where the tests live (story type: Integration, with UI and Visual/Feel parts).* Boarding has no code (BS-1), so there is no unit under test: every (auto) row is an **integration test** that drives the owners' real code (Attention's integrator, Interaction's handlers, Suit, Assembly, Save/Load, the Graph) through a headless script plus a Web Map stub, and belongs in `tests/integration/boarding-and-salvage/` (test names `test_boarding_<scenario>_<expected>`). The UI and visual rows need **retained screenshots in `production/qa/evidence/boarding/`**; they are blocking and are not waived at `qa.level: minimal`. **Do not re-implement an owner's formula in a Boarding test:** rows that assert a number from D.1 to D.10 read the value out of the owner's running code under the fixture (Attention's `attention_integrator`, the Graph's leak step, Web Map's `hop_fuel`, Sanity's drift) and compare it with the golden value printed here, so a retune of a placeholder updates the expected number and not the test's logic. Every duration is in ticks at `TICK_RATE` 60; every Attention figure is the run-quantised integer recomputed from `attention_integrator` (mAU) with the owners' [PLACEHOLDER] values (`U_TOOL` 4, `U_LOUD` 180, `TAU_ATT_TICKS` 1800, `ATT_THRESHOLD` 6000, ring-1 `B_table` 300). Route figures use the assumed anchors of D.1 and are regenerated when Layout's `route_length_px` lands (AC-BS-5). The headless fixtures use Web Map's command through a **WM stub** that implements `pod_hop` as a body transfer with the published leg and settle ticks, because the Chart Table and the pod body are not part of Boarding's test. **The reference script runs with fixture dwells of 0 (`REF_ZERO_DWELL`, AC-BS-6, exactly 3612 ticks at the pre-ruling 13-tile anchor, and the `T_sortie_floor` that D.1 computes from the published anchors once Derelict Authoring lengthens the decision route); the noise golden schedule of D.2 (`REF_GOLDEN`) adds a 12-tick pickup dwell and a 30-tick console approach, over the same 60 door ticks (AC-BS-14).** *Observer protocol for every (manual) row:* a response counts when the observer, using a pre-agreed phrase list, records a spoken reference to the named concept within 10 s of the trigger; two observers code independently and a disagreement counts as not met. Manual thresholds are the provisional MVP thresholds `[PROVISIONAL: creative-director]` (creative-director ruling, stage 12: adopted as written) until a second round of eight or more testers promotes them. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Two fixtures, named.** **`REF_ZERO_DWELL`** is the reference script with every dwell and every unregistered TAP or swap duration carried as 0 (AC-BS-6: exactly 3612 ticks at the pre-ruling anchor, recomputed from the published anchors after the ruled route lands). **`REF_GOLDEN`** is the generated golden-schedule file of D.2 (walk-in 312, doors 60 idle, cut 240, pickup dwell 12, carry 624, console approach 30) with its variants and with the `REF_ZERO_DWELL` schedule; it is produced by running the schedules against the owners' running code and is never typed. They share the route and differ only in dwell, so a figure from one is never quoted as the other. **Snapshot rule.** Every figure printed in a row below that rests on a [PLACEHOLDER] is a *snapshot at the defaults*: the test reads its expected value from `REF_GOLDEN` or from the owner's running code and compares it with the live constants, the printed number is documentation, and AC-BS-114's range sweep is what proves no row hard-codes it. The independent **oracle cases** (D.2 O-1 to O-3, D.3's abort arithmetic) are computed by hand and are never read from `REF_GOLDEN`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**Row classes (game behaviour versus document conformance versus contract).** *G* game behaviour of the composition (Boarding-owned integration). *C* contract tests: the row mirrors an owner's acceptance, runs in the sortie fixture and cites the owner's AC id, which the tooling story's resolver script fills in; a C row whose owner AC cannot be resolved becomes a G row. *D* document conformance (the text and figures of this GDD). *R* report or advisory. *V* retained evidence (screenshot). *M* manual. *X* deferred, blocked on a spike, or a spike. **G:** 4, 6, 8, 14a, 14b, 14c, 25, 27, 31, 34, 48, 51b, 56, 82, 84, 92, 119, 123, 127, 128, 130, 131. **C:** 10, 11a, 11b, 11c, 12b, 13a, 13b, 13c, 15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 20, 21, 23, 24, 28, 29, 32, 33a, 35, 36, 37, 39, 40, 47a, 50, 57a, 57b, 58a, 58b, 58c, 58d, 58e, 58f, 59, 60, 63, 66, 81, 83, 85, 86, 87, 88, 89, 90, 91, 93, 94, 95, 102, 103, 107, 108, 115, 116, 117, 120. **D:** 1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 9, 12a, 22, 30, 42, 47b, 49a, 49b, 52, 64, 65, 67, 68, 96, 101. **R:** 41, 53, 54, 55, 104, 106, 109, 111, 113, 114. **V:** 47c, 61, 62, 97, 98, 99, 100. **M:** 12c, 69 to 78, 122, 124, 125, 126. **X:** 33b, 38, 43, 44, 45, 46, 79, 80, 110, 112, 118, 129. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**Test owners and sequencing.**

| Order | Story (type) | Owner | Delivers | Needs first |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | **TS-BS-0 tooling** (Integration) | qa-lead with the gameplay-programmer | the headless harness, the WM stub (`pod_hop` as a body transfer with the published leg and settle ticks), the `REF_ZERO_DWELL` and `REF_GOLDEN` generators, the allow-list generator of AC-BS-3, the stale-token lint with its seeded-defect fixture (AC-BS-64), the owner-AC resolver for the C rows | none |
| 1 | Reference sortie (Integration) | gameplay-programmer; systems-designer reviews `REF_GOLDEN` | the G rows except 51a, 51b, 92 and 123 | TS-BS-0 and the Interaction, Suit, Assembly and Web Map stories |
| 2 | Contract tests (Integration) | each neighbour's story owner; qa-lead schedules | the C rows | the neighbour's story and TS-BS-0 |
| 3 | Dark-dock invariant (Integration) | gameplay-programmer with the level-designer | AC-BS-51b, AC-BS-92, AC-BS-78 | Emitter State's fixture class, Web Map's acceptance and Derelict Authoring's placement lint (the OQ-BS-4 ruling) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| 4 | Tell decision (Integration) | systems-designer with the game-designer | AC-BS-123, AC-BS-122, AC-BS-72, the tell clause of AC-BS-14b | AC-BS-123 green with the boarding spike's pass line (AC-BS-132; Attention's recalibration is run, F-26) and Derelict Authoring's lengthened route *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* |
| 5 | Evidence (UI, Visual) | ux-designer and art-director | the V rows | the HUD and art stories |
| 6 | Playtest (manual) | producer with the qa-lead | the M rows | stories 3 and 4 are preconditions |

### A. Composition and the zero footprint

- **AC-BS-1 (lint)** GIVEN the final GDD and `design/registry/entities.yaml` diffed before and after the Boarding stage WHEN the text is scanned for a declared state block, step hook, command type, mutator, read view, notification or registry constant THEN none is declared by Boarding & Salvage; the only registry changes are `referenced_by` entries and the design-time formula entries sourced here (`route_ticks`, `sortie_noise_budget`, `tell_window_fit`, `hazard_dwell_budget`, `cap_versus_leave`, `modules_per_fuel`, `check_cost_ratio`, `sortie_dwell_envelope`, `loss_window_after_cut`, `lamp_cell_budget`); every registry constant name the GDD uses has a different `source:` file; and `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `DROP_OFFSET_PX` and `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` are cited as the owners' constants and not minted (`OPEN_END_RATE_PM` is the Graph's registry entry, with Boarding in `referenced_by` only). [BS-1]
- **AC-BS-2 (review)** GIVEN each of BS-1 to BS-24 THEN the rule either ends in an owner citation or is one of the ten composition rules the Format paragraph lists (BS-2, BS-5, BS-8, BS-12, BS-13, BS-14, BS-15, BS-17, BS-19, BS-24); the reviewer reports any other uncited rule. [BS-1]
- **AC-BS-3 (lint)** GIVEN every hook, command, mutator, read-view and notification identifier in the GDD WHEN each is resolved against **an allow-list generated by script from the owners' published lists** (kernel CR-3 and CR-8, Interaction's verb and command table, the Graph's M1 to M18 and hook ids, Web Map's `pod_hop`, Assembly's commands, Suit's and Controller's mutators, Sanity's `record_falsification`) THEN there are zero unresolved identifiers, or each unresolved one carries an ask id. The list is never typed into this row. [BS-1]
- **AC-BS-4 (auto)** GIVEN the headless reference sortie of AC-BS-6 WHEN the registrations and the step log are read THEN Boarding registers 0 hooks and 0 state blocks and contributes 0 dirty keys and 0 bytes of snapshot state. (The timing half is AC-BS-111, advisory.) [BS-1]
